====================================================================================================

Snakes on a plate: Could reptile consumption have freed us to settle down?
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
15:28:25 UTC
00:28:25 KST

The snake slithered frantically into the underbrush, but it was too slow. It had been spotted and its fate was sealed. It had become a statistic.
Marks of stone tools on bones of reptiles unearthed at the el-Wad Cave on Israel's Mount Carmel show that the hunter-gatherer Natufians living there 12,000 to 15,000 years ago routinely ate snakes and legless lizards. This was the conclusion in a paper published last week in PNAS by University of Haifa researchers Ma'ayan Lev, Mina Weinstein-Evron and Reuven Yeshurun.
The question underlying the research had been the degree prehistoric peoples exploited such squamates for food. Reptile and tortoise remains have been identified in various archaeological circumstances, but the scale of the phenomenon was unclear.
Latter-day hunter-gatherer societies consume reptiles in desert climes and the American and Asian tropics. It's a bit niche, but in Florida, people hunt down and eat invasive Burmese pythons, though experts warn that one may want to analyze the dearly departed for heavy metal contamination before chowing down.
In other words, reptile is routinely eaten among peoples who are either crazed by TikTok or whose choices are severely constrained. Snake reportedly tastes like chicken, but where modern people have their druthers, they don't go there.
But how did ancient peoples in eras before animal husbandry view the scaly set?
Similarly to today: somewhat askance, perhaps. The evidence suggests that snakes and the like were not a favored food, not for the Natufians nor for anybody else. The degree of reptile consumption seems to depend on what else the people could catch. But the Natufians' reptile consumption was significant, almost a third of their animal intake in the Early Natufian stage at el-Wad.
In fact, squamate on the plate may exhibit not only their dietary flexibility. It may have even contributed to their settling down, the researchers suggest. How is that?
See snakes, can settle
The Natufian culture in the Levant predated agriculture. These hunter-gatherers broke the paradigm that farming must have emerged before permanent settlement. Evidently, it didn't. Thousands of years before the first wheat seed was deliberately planted or the first herbivore was domesticated, Natufian hunter-gatherers had left the cave life behind and settled down.
At el-Wad, in the Nahal Me'arot Nature Reserve, the evidence shows three phases of Early Natufian settlement: pre-architectural, architectural and post-architectural.
In other words, these folks didn't stride out of the cave, matted heads held high, and start to build stone houses. How did they live outside at el-Wad, for instance, before they built houses? "Maybe in tents," Yeshurun speculates over the phone with Haaretz. Or in simple huts, like separate work detected at Ohalo by the Sea of Galilee from more than 20,000 years ago.
But live there they did, and they continued to forage and hunt, as their ancestors had done before, possibly with the help of tame dogs, which we know they had. Hunting was how they gained their protein; they didn't have domesticated goats or cows. What did they hunt?
Some degree of reptile acquisition could have been predicted. Unrelated work at Tel Aviv University has demonstrated that over the last 1.5 million years, the average body mass of animals in the southern Levant shrank by more than 98 percent. Where once our species and ancestors preyed on megafauna, by the time of the Natufians, the biggest game left in town was gazelles and deer.
Archaeology of the Natufians has shown that this is exactly what they preferred to eat; gazelles comprised anywhere from 40 percent to 80 percent of the animals they consumed, going by bone finds. Now we learn that the Natufians did not shrink from snake.
In fact, they ate reptiles to a degree suggesting that this practice enabled their very settlement, freeing them of the need to roam far in search for more attractive protein, like gazelle. They could stay in their houses all year; the climate was gorgeous and the nutrition was right there for the taking. But would they have eaten the snakes if they had other options?
At the initial settlement stage, one finds some reptile consumption. Exploitation of reptiles increased during the architectural phase, to as much of 40 percent of the acquired animals. Then reptile consumption receded again, the researchers concluded after analyzing thousands of reptile bones throughout the 20-layer archaeological sequence at el-Wad Terrace, all of which date to the Natufians.
Actually, to what degree does el-Wad represent all Natufians, a culture that lasted around 3,000 years and was dispersed throughout the Levant? In terms of reptile finds and a few other aspects, el-Wad is the most detailed case study we have of this culture, Yeshurun explains.
He adds that not only did the Natufians destroy the paradigm of agriculture before settlement; in their voraciousness, they also disrupted the natural distribution of snakes, frogs and so on. Before them, squamate and amphibian distribution was dictated solely by geography. But Natufian sites are consistently dominated by large squamates; a Natufian site on the Carmel is like one at the Hula Valley to the north, for example, despite the geographical distance.
So yes, el-Wad can be taken as representative and it has the best record of cut marks and manipulation of reptile remains in the Epipaleolithic. Lev, who led the research, identified marks on 82 vertebrae, in roughly the same location on the bone, suggesting culinary attention. Note that a cut mark on snake bones refers to lesions on vertebrae because snakes don't have bones below the neck.
Okay. El-Wad represents Natufian culture. The reptiles the el-Wadders targeted were the biggest and slowest, the researchers found: the European glass lizard (which is legless and looks like a snake), the large whip snake (which is a snake), and the eastern Montpellier snake (also a snake).
The legless lizard appears in all the Natufian layers but the others appear mainly in the architectural phase.
Seeking to explain why the legless lizard was found in all layers, while other equally edible squamates were confined to the most intensely occupied layer, Yeshurun consulted a herpetologist (a reptile and amphibian expert). Could the legless lizard might have been attracted to human habitation? But commensal relationships aren't a thing with reptiles, the expert said, which brings us back to options.
Reptiles may have been part of the total Natufian subsistence package, but the intensification of their consumption after the Natufians settled down and built homes suggests that the other options had run out. Or away.
Note that to this day northern Israel is thronged with wild boars, some of which are bold enough to live in modern cities. Nor have gazelles and deer gone extinct. (Though a host of other decently sized animals such as the hartebeest and hippopotamus most definitely have gone extinct locally – and much later than many assume. They only disappeared forever more as wars raged over the land during the biblical period.)
So why didn't the Natufians eat many boars? Why resort to snakes, which may taste like chicken but hardly have any of the fatty layers that humans crave?
Slow snakes are relatively easy to acquire, while swine (and even deer) are smart enough to learn to avoid areas with permanent, hungry human habitation. Note that the boars living in Haifa and other Israeli cities are not subject to predation pressure, because we have supermarkets and can't be bothered to chase and kill them, so they don't need to learn to avoid us.
But back at el-Wad, the longer the Natufians stayed there, the more they had to rely on the animals that didn't have the smarts to hoof it.
The Malthusian Natufian
In the post-architectural phase of the Natufian culture, the people seem to have reverted to seasonal occupation of el-Wad. It isn't that they necessarily reverted to total nomadism, Yeshurun notes, but they did abandon the permanent settlement. "I think it was a very local thing," he says. "At el-Wad, we attribute it to the demographic cycle."
The model of a settlement is that it starts small; a few people in a great environment surrounded by food. After a few generations, the settlement reaches the maximum population that the environment can sustain, the population advance stops as food grows scarce and conflicts develop, and then some folks move away, some kill each other and, long story short, we have abandonment.
At el-Wad that cycle took several centuries at most, Yeshurun says, after which Natufians continued to live sporadically at the site for another 2,000 years.
We don't know why the architectural phase collapsed, but plausibly, even without the local serpent population imploding or fleeing, the environment couldn't sustain enough food for a growing sedentary population. Large animals hadn't gone entirely extinct but may have come under regional hunting pressure. It wouldn't pay for a sedentary Natufian to trek far away to hunt down a hartebeest and lug the cadaver home.
Peak snake consumption was during the middle phase, the architectural one. All this supports the thesis that the Natufians chose to settle in a lush environment that became progressively less so and ate prolifically of the legless lizards, the snakes, and other small animals like hares when they had no choice. Yeshurun adds that the snakes they targeted were not only slow and large but nonvenomous – there isn't evidence that they doted on viper.
After the Natufians and the collapse of their settlement endeavor, what happened? Agriculture started to emerge. In the pre-pottery Neolithic, we find the first stabs at orderly grain cultivation and animal husbandry, while people continued to hunt. But as cows, goat, wheat lentils and the like joined the menu, nobody needed to resort to snake anymore.

Europol Warns of Violent Shift in European Museum Heists
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
15:28:00 UTC
00:28:00 KST

Reported by
Europol is warning of an alarming shift in museum heists across Europe, as criminal networks abandon traditional stealth in favor of brute force and extreme violence, according to a report published Monday.
The European Union’s law enforcement agency said perpetrators are increasingly ditching the traditional “deception and discreet execution” of art theft, historically viewed as a “non-violent form of organized property crime,” in favor of direct and confrontational methods.
Instead of niche experts, Europol’s report—”Changing Tactics, Changing Targets: The Evolving Nature of Museum Heists”—found that these new heists are often carried out by decentralized groups of opportunistic criminals recruited via social media on a “crime-as-a-service” basis. These new criminal profiles are increasingly armed with sledgehammers, axes, crowbars, and explosives and firearms to breach premises, smash display cases and intimidate or assault staff in broad daylight.
Recent high-profile incidents illustrate this violent shift. In November 2024, four suspects armed with baseball bats and axes stormed the Cognacq-Jay art museum in France during opening hours, smashing a display case to steal seven gold and diamond items before escaping on scooters.
In January 2025, thieves detonated explosives to blow off the doors of the Drents Museum in Assen, the Netherlands, to steal a 2,500-year-old Dacian gold helmet and three gold bracelets. Dutch police, with the help of an art detective, successfully recovered the helmet and two of the bracelets in April 2026 after a lengthy investigation.
A similar violent attack occurred at the Louvre Museum in Paris in October 2025, where thieves broke a window using a mechanical ladder, threatened guards and visitors, and smashed display cases in the Apollo Gallery to steal nine items of jewelry worth an estimated 88 million euros ($102.69 million).
Alongside the changing tactics, Europol noted that organized crime groups are shifting their focus away from traditional art and toward raw materials, increasingly targeting gold, silver, and gemstone-laden jewelry due to their rising market value.
Precious metals can be easily melted down to erase trace evidence for law enforcement and facilitate illicit sales during the fencing process, the agency explained, adding that precious stones can be easily removed and resold.
While the October 2025 heist at the Louvre Museum made headlines around the world, that jewelry theft was actually part of an accelerating trend across France. A month earlier, a Chinese national—already known to police—stole 7 kilograms of gold nuggets valued at 1.5 million euros ($1.75 million) from the National Museum of Natural History in Paris. French media outlets reported that she was arrested a few days later at Barcelona airport carrying a kilogram of melted gold and was placed under formal investigation for “theft as part of an organized gang.”
This summer, France saw a spate of other high-profile museum thefts. First, on July 1, an archaeological center was targeted in a robbery where approximately 40 Roman gold coins valued at 120,000 euros ($139,994) were stolen. Only four days later, sledgehammer-armed robbers broke into the Lalique Museum and fled with 27 jewelry items valued at 4.5 million euros ($5.25 million), while on July 24, a priceless, 2,500-year-old Celtic gold collar was stolen in broad daylight from the Musée du Pays Châtillonnais.

Ukraine faces €23B defense funding hole as Western cash lags
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
15:27:38 UTC
00:27:38 KST

Volodymyr Zelenskyy welcomes international leaders upon their arrival for the Coalition of the Willing meeting, in Kyiv on Aug. 24, 2026. | Pool photo by Henry Nicholls via AFP/Getty Images
KYIV — Ukraine faces a €23 billion hole in its defense budget this year, leaving Kyiv scrambling for cash to fight Russia even as Western leaders gathered in the capital to mark the country's independence day and pledge ongoing support.
The money is needed not only for weapons and air defense, but also to pay soldiers, support the families of those killed and ensure Ukraine has the supplies it needs to start 2027.
“We are not asking [you] to fight for us. Give Ukraine everything to stop this war, to stop it faster than Putin can start the next one,” President Volodymyr Zelenskyy said in a speech Monday.
Advertisement
U.K. Prime Minister Andy Burnham was in Kyiv Monday for a meeting of the so-called coalition of the willing.
European Council President António Costa, Luxembourgish Prime Minister Luc Frieden, Moldovan President Maia Sandu, Estonian President Alar Karis, Finnish President Alexander Stubb and other Nordic and Baltic leaders also attended in person, while French President Emmanuel Macron and German Chancellor Friedrich Merz joined remotely.
“Ukrainian people deserve not just continued support but our gratitude ... I am determined we [the coalition of the willing] will work together to deliver the support you need,” Burnham said in a statement.
The funding gap originated after the defense ministry brought forward spending originally planned for the second half of the year, while the EU’s €90 billion loan was delayed by roadblocks from Hungary.
“At the beginning of the year, decisions were made at the ministry to use in the first half of the year funds that were intended to be disbursed in the second half," Zelenskyy said during a meeting with reporters on Saturday. The shortfall comes on top of €30 billion in funding Europe has already pledged to Ukraine this year as part of the €90 billion loan.
Purchases made included "fiber-optic drones, middle-strike drones, interceptors,” former Defense Minister Mykhailo Fedorov told POLITICO in June.
Advertisement
"Now, in the second half of the year, those funds need to be compensated for and supplemented,” Zelenskyy added.
The European Commission offered some relief Monday, approving €6.1 billion in new defense procurement for Ukraine. This latest disbursement from the EU’s €90 billion loan package will fund air-defense missiles and ammunition, as well as radars — among Kyiv’s most urgent needs. But the money falls far short of what Ukraine says it requires.
Ukraine needs about €6.8 billion to €8.5 billion to ensure a “normal start” to next year, the Ukrainian leader said. That money is effectively needed in advance to pay for weapons and other supplies for January 2027. Around €17 billion more is needed for military salaries, payments to the families of fallen soldiers and other expenses.
“A lot of money is needed,” he added.
But domestic politics are also creating obstacles to international funding. Zelenskyy admitted that Ukrainian lawmakers are stalling the implementation of reforms on which some international aid is conditional.
Overall, parliament is currently delaying 24 reform bills tied to Western funding disbursement. While not all the funds are tied to defense spending, getting EU money would free up Ukraine's scarce resources for other urgent defense needs.
Advertisement
Last week, the Ukrainian parliament’s law enforcement committee removed five government and judicial reform bills from consideration because they are no longer relevant, Ukrainian MP Anastasiya Radina said last week.
Maksym Buzhanskiy, a lawmaker from Zelenskyy's party, defended the committee's decision to remove the bills, claiming they do not correspond to Ukraine commitments needed for EU funding.
Zelenskyy is planning to ask for disbursement of EU funds ahead of schedule.
“I am working on this with the French and the Germans. Practically, we need to bring forward the second portion of the European funds allocated for Ukraine, and this is an extremely difficult task,” Zelenskyy said.

West turns Ukraine into 'frontier country' making money on war — economist
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
15:26:30 UTC
00:26:30 KST

MOSCOW, August 24. /TASS/. The West has transformed Ukraine into a "frontier nation," whose sole purpose appears to be waging war while relying heavily on foreign aid, according to Oleg Pendzin, executive director of the Ukrainian Economic Discussion Club, who was speaking in an interview with the UNIAN news agency.
"From day one of the conflict, we've been fighting on borrowed money," Pendzin stated. "The socio-political climate surrounding us is causing significant concerns among our European partners regarding their own futures. Essentially, they are buying off Ukrainian blood."
Pendzin pointed out that Europe seems intent on shifting the entire burden of the conflict onto Ukrainian shoulders by funneling money and weapons into the country. He described Ukraine as a "frontier country" that exists solely through financial injections, aid, mercenaries, and weaponry - serving as a shield rather than an independent nation.
"Ukraine cannot survive solely on its own resources at this point," he emphasized. "Imagine if we didn't have access to the recent 90 billion euros from the EU lending program - our entire social system would collapse instantly. There would be no social assistance, no salaries for public sector workers, no aid for the impoverished, no pensions."
For several years, Ukraine's budget has been plagued by a record deficit. Domestic revenues no longer fully cover even military expenses, with Western aid filling the gap for most other expenditures. Kiev openly admits that its own financial resources are exhausted, and securing additional funding is increasingly challenging. Meanwhile, Western partners, including the IMF, are urging Ukrainian authorities to explore new avenues of self-financing, such as tightening tax policies. As former Ukrainian Prime Minister Nikolay Azarov (2010-2014) warned, without continued Western lending, Ukraine risks ceasing to exist as a functioning state.

Ex-Pussy Riot Member Says She Helped the FSB Spy on Kremlin Critics
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
15:23:05 UTC
00:23:05 KST

Former Pussy Riot member Rita Flores has revealed that she was coerced into working as an informant for Russia’s FSB security service, saying that she gathered information on anti-war activists and was involved in a failed assassination plot against an exiled Kremlin critic.
Flores said FSB officers coerced her into signing a cooperation agreement following a February 2023 raid on her Moscow apartment, threatening her with criminal prosecution over social media posts about alleged Russian war crimes in Ukraine alongside death threats against her relatives.
“They began recruiting me with all kinds of threats and abuse [including] threats to murder my family and that I’d never be able to prove who killed my mother,” she told the exiled news outlet Port in a video interview.
Operating under the codename “Harley,” Flores said she was assigned to build detailed profiles on Russian artists and political dissidents. Between early 2024 and June, she received monthly payments of 50,000 rubles ($600) from her security service handlers after an initial disbursement of 30,000 rubles ($360).
Flores detailed how she deliberately sabotaged her FSB assignments for over two years, faking personal feuds with targets like artists Philippenzo and Pavel Krisevich to delay their arrest by law enforcement authorities.
In mid-2025, Flores said she was ordered to lure her ex-boyfriend and former Pussy Riot member Pyotr Verzilov into a cafe in Serbia where undercover FSB agents planned to kidnap and kill him. The activist said she was offered 3 million rubles ($36,000) for her role in that assassination plot.
“That was the point of no return. Before, I thought I just needed to wait to get my passport back and flee. But then I realized how far they were willing to go,” Flores said in the interview with Port.
Refusing to help carry out the assassination plot against Verzilov, Flores stalled her handlers and sought help from human rights lawyer Dmitry Zakhvatov. Exiled businessman Leonid Nevzlin subsequently helped her flee Russia earlier this month.
The Moscow Times could not independently verify any of the claims made by Flores.

Music Review: Mike D 5D’s debut solo album ‘Thank You’ offers glitchy, post-Beastie Boy fun
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
15:23:02 UTC
00:23:02 KST

Music Review: Mike D 5D’s debut solo album ‘Thank You’ offers glitchy, post-Beastie Boy fun
ATLANTA (AP) — Mike D 5D, a new project from former Beastie Boy Michael Diamond, will release “Thank You” on Friday — the debut solo album from the MC and the first new music from the Beastie Boys’ camp since the 2012 death of Adam “MCA” Yauch.
“Thank You” is a debut album unafraid to traverse new sonic ground — and suffers only the occasional misfire.
Joined by his band — his sons Skyler and Davis Diamond as well as Eddie Ruscha, Will Graefe and Milo Ruscha — Mike D’s album bristles with stuttering drum and bass breaks and distressed vocals on the opening tracks “Switch Up” and “What We Got,” both of which sound as if they were pushed through an overdrive pedal. Somehow it works and is fitting with Mike D 5D’s high-volume and energetic style.
Less necessary are meandering and repetitive songs such as “Secrets Pt. II,” in which Mike D discusses his discomfort. “I got secrets,” the rapper implores, repeatedly — no, seriously, a lot — while failing to reveal one. The rapper has a musical idea, but not much of a lyrical one.
Much of the album plays out like a man in a bedroom with a vintage synth, a half dozen beered-up friends and a laptop stuffed with aging drum software. Such is true of the track “Crypto,” which purports to cast a cautious eye at greed, but lurches melodically. Ultimately, it relents to a frenetic and fuzzy declaration from Mike D of “All these people in your ear, they’re in your ear / All these people talking in your ear, in your ear” — which apparently means something to him.
But overwhelmingly, “Thank You” is messy, noisy glitch-rap fun. Consider “I Don’t Care,” one of the top tracks. It holds a down-tempo pace and finds Mike D weighing the balance of responsibilities and their emotional returns: “We all have a role / I didn’t see mine,” he sings. “Start with a goal get lost in the deadline.” The track excels thanks to wonderful Mini Korg synth work from Hubert Blanc-Fracard, formerly one half of the French music duo Cassius.
Despite a dash of low lights, “Thank You” is refreshingly inventive. That in itself is an accomplishment. To have the aim and drive to create something this new and far afield deserves applause. He’s reaching beyond the low rung recreations of Beastie Boy flavor he could have easily leaned into. But that’s easy and accessible and where’s the fun in that?
Credit is due here for exploration and commitment. He’s Mike D and he gets respect.
___
“Thank You,” by Mike D 5D
Three stars out of five
On repeat: “I Don’t Care”
Skip it: “Crypto”
For fans of: A distressed “Paul’s Boutique”

Deportees who refused to exit a US flight in Liberia are sent to Equatorial Guinea

Deportees who refused to exit a US flight in Liberia are sent to Equatorial Guinea
DAKAR, Senegal (AP) — Six people who were deported by the United States to Liberia last week resisted getting off the plane and instead were sent to Equatorial Guinea, another African nation that signed a third-country deportation agreement with the Trump administration, a person on the plane and a lawyer in contact with the six said Monday.
Under a series of often secret agreements, the Trump administration has deported thousands of people to two dozen countries that aren’t their own as it pushes ahead with its immigration crackdown, advocates say. Immigration lawyers say the practice is being used as a legal loophole to indirectly return some asylum seekers to countries they fled.
The group who refused to get off the plane in Liberia on Thursday include four Cuban men, a Brazilian man and a Cameroonian woman, the person and lawyer said.
A Honduran deportee aboard the flight said the group refused to disembark, saying Liberia was “not their country” and that they would face danger there. They were then separated from the others, he said. He spoke on condition of anonymity out of fear of reprisals.
He said the other passengers got off in Liberia because they feared officers would force them.
Meredyth Yoon, litigation director for Asian Americans Advancing Justice-Atlanta, said the deportees who resisted leaving the plane in Liberia were told they would be returned to the United States but instead were flown to Malabo city in Equatorial Guinea.
“It was a really heartbreaking and chaotic scene when they arrived in Malabo on Thursday,” Yoon said.
Some arrived in chains and in extreme distress, she said, including the Cameroonian woman and one of the Cuban men, who Yoon said has a large abdominal growth and uses a colostomy bag that must be drained regularly.
There is no medical staff at the hotel in Equatorial Guinea where the six are being held, alongside around 40 other deported migrants, she added.
Conditions at the hotel have worsened in recent months, Yoon said, with food sometimes withheld and guards accused of threatening, beating and choking some of the migrants.
Equatorial Guinea’s government did not immediately respond to a request seeking comment.
Under an opaque $7.5 million deal with the Trump administration, Equatorial Guinea’s all-powerful president, Teodoro Obiang Nguema Mbasogo, has turned the hotel owned by his family into a prison for asylum seekers deported from the U.S.

Denmark’s prime minister blasts unsolicited beauty advice from plastic surgeon on Instagram
COPENHAGEN, Denmark (AP) — Danish Prime Minister Mette Frederiksen is fed up with getting unsolicited beauty advice.
The 48-year-old wrote Monday on Instagram that she had just returned back home from a trip to Ukraine, where she was exposed to air raid sirens in the ongoing war, only to find a message by a plastic surgeon offering her to “fix that little spot on your nose.”
“I’ve lost count of how many messages I’ve received over the years about my appearance,” Frederiksen wrote. “Especially that I have ugly teeth and thin hair. That I have an ugly ‘wart’ on my nose and well-meaning advice to get some Botox.”
The prime minister of the Nordic country posted a screen shot of the plastic surgeon’s offer on Instagram but without identifying him.
She also posted her response: “Oh no. I’m happy with my body — I really don’t need a male plastic surgeon to have an opinion about it!”
Frederiksen, a Social Democrat and Denmark ’s second female prime minister, is now in her third term, making her one of Europe’s longest-serving and most experienced leaders. She has led the European Union and NATO member country since mid-2019.
She has pledged to maintain a firm stance against U.S. President Donald Trump’s ambitions for Greenland, the kingdom’s semiautonomous territory, and continue supporting Ukraine against Russia’s war of aggression.
On Instagram, Frederiksen stressed that she does not interfere with other people’s looks and that “I’m perfectly fine with my body — as it is when you’re a woman approaching 50.”
She added that “if you’re going to write to me (and you’re more than welcome to), I hope you’ll use your time to talk about something more meaningful. Not my nose.”
The prime minister also said that those unsolicited beauty tips and suggestions made her feel “indignant” and that “today, I’m sending an extra loving thought to all of you who have to put up with the ‘perfect imperfections’ we all walk around with every day.”

Gang coalition unleashes violent new attack on farming community in Haiti
Unknown
Unknown Publisher

00:23:02 KST

Gang coalition unleashes violent new attack on farming community in Haiti
PORT-AU-PRINCE, Haiti (AP) — A powerful gang coalition attacked a once peaceful farming community nestled in the hills above Haiti’s capital, killing multiple people, an official told The Associated Press on Monday.
Kenscoff Mayor Jean Massillon said armed men raided the community late Sunday, setting fire to homes and killing residents.
“It is still impossible to establish a definitive death toll due to access difficulties and communication problems,” he said.
Wails filled the air in Kenscoff as survivors found the bodies of friends and family strewn on the streets early Monday.
Farmer Mathurin Pierre, 41, told The Associated Press that he lost 14 loved ones and that an unknown number remain missing.
Massillon blamed the attack on members of Viv Ansanm, which the U.S. government designated as a foreign terrorist organization last year.
“They sowed chaos,” Massillon said.
He said gang members targeted a church and homes including those owned by Dr. Jean “Bill” Pape. He is a renowned doctor who runs a non-governmental organization in Haiti that helps patients including those with HIV and tuberculosis.
No further details about the attack were available. A spokesperson for the U.N.-backed gang suppression force did not immediately return a message seeking comment.
Gangs control an estimated 70% of Port-au-Prince, the capital, with Viv Ansanm repeatedly targeting nearby Kenscoff.
Gang violence has displaced a record 1.5 million people across Haiti, where more than 3,100 people were reported killed from January to June and another 1,189 injured, according to U.N. statistics.
The attack comes less than a week after the U.S. government deported more than 160 people to the troubled Caribbean country for the first time since the Trump administration won a legal battle to end Temporary Protected Status for some 350,000 Haitians.
Haiti’s government has said some of the deportees included TPS holders. The U.S. Department of Homeland Security said Monday in a statement to the AP that those deported on Friday included several gang members with criminal records ranging from assault to drug trafficking.
Haiti is scheduled to hold general elections for the first time in more than a decade on Dec. 13, and it has been registering voters in Port-au-Prince and beyond. Experts have warned it’s currently too dangerous to hold elections.
___
Associated Press reporter Dánica Coto in San Juan, Puerto Rico, contributed.
___
Follow AP’s coverage of Latin America and the Caribbean at https://apnews.com/hub/latin-america

2 UN peacekeepers killed in an ambush in South Sudan
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
15:23:02 UTC
00:23:02 KST

2 UN peacekeepers killed in an ambush in South Sudan
JUBA, South Sudan (AP) — Two United Nations peacekeepers were killed on Monday when armed men ambushed a patrol traveling to a remote area of South Sudan, according to the United Nations Mission in South Sudan.
Others injured in the attack by unidentified people included three peacekeepers, two members of the South Sudanese police and two civilian staff of the mission, UNMISS said in a statement.
The mission said it had deployed a “Quick Reaction Force” to secure the area and support the emergency response after the attack. The patrol was going to the area of Pajut in the state of Jonglei when it came under attack.
“Such deadly violence targeting UNMISS personnel who are serving to support peace in South Sudan is unacceptable,” Anita Kiki Gbeho, the head of UNMISS, said in the statement.
The identities and nationalities of the two peacekeepers killed were not immediately disclosed.
Violence is rampant in South Sudan, where government troops have been combating fighters and militias loyal to opposition leader Riek Machar, who was suspended last year as vice president after he faced charges of treason and other offenses he says are politically motivated. Attacks against peacekeepers are rare.
A 2018 peace deal between President Salva Kiir and Machar, who is under house arrest, has collapsed, leading to fears of a return to full-blown civil war.
More than 450 incidents of violence against humanitarian workers and assets were reported between January and July, more than double the number recorded during the same period last year, according to U.N. figures.
The conflict escalated in December when opposition forces seized government outposts in Jonglei. The government has conducted a counteroffensive since January with aerial bombardments and ground assaults, despite an official commitment to the peace agreement.
The U.S. has urged talks between Kiir and Machar. Earlier this year it called for “urgent action to uphold peace and mitigate the risk of civil war through an immediate return to dialogue among the parties to the 2018 peace agreement.”

Former world snooker champion Graeme Dott convicted of sexually abusing 2 small children

Former world snooker champion Graeme Dott convicted of sexually abusing 2 small children
LONDON (AP) — Former world snooker champion Graeme Dott was convicted Monday of sexually abusing two primary school children in Scotland in the 1990s and early 2000s.
Dott, 49, was found guilty of abusing a girl from 1993-96 and a boy from 2006-10 after a five-day trial at the High Court in Glasgow. The judge ordered that Dott be held in custody ahead of his sentencing on Sept. 29.
The woman, now in her 40s, testified that Dott sexually abused her on multiple occasions, “caressing” her bottom over her clothing, exposing himself and touching her with parts of his body including his penis.
The man, now in his late 20s, said he was abused on multiple occasions, with Dott touching him inappropriately, kissing him and performing sexual acts.
Snooker’s governing body suspended Dott in April 2025 after he was charged in the case. He was ranked No. 56 in the world at the time.
Dott won the world snooker championship in 2006.

Firefighters are trying to stop a fast-moving Nevada wildfire from spreading to homes in Reno
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
15:23:02 UTC
00:23:02 KST

Firefighters are trying to stop a fast-moving Nevada wildfire from spreading to homes in Reno
Live from Reno, Nevada, as firefighters try to bring the fast-moving Hawk Fire under control. Some 90,000 people have been told to evacuate as officials said the fire was “human-caused,” without providing details.
RENO, Nevada (AP) — A wildfire that mushroomed over the weekend in the Sierra Nevada foothills spread perilously close to neighborhoods in Reno, Nevada, forcing authorities to urge more than 90,000 residents to leave their homes.
The out-of-control fire has already burned some homes, but there’s concern that shifting winds could push the flames into newly developed and densely packed neighborhoods on the edge of Nevada’s second-biggest city.
Schools across the county were closed Monday because of the evacuations and to keep roads clear for emergency crews.
Authorities said late Sunday that the Hawk Fire had stayed in its footprint and firefighters were extinguishing hotspots while working to keep the fire from jumping a main highway and reaching areas filled with homes and businesses.
The fire began Saturday and grew to more than 23 square miles (60 square kilometers) on Sunday across foothills ringed by neighborhoods near the California state line. There was zero containment.
At least six people — three first responders and three civilians — were injured. Officials said the fire was “human-caused” but did not say whether it was intentionally set or accidental.
Videos posted on social media showed the smoldering ruins of homes and cars, with only a scorched basketball hoop still standing.
Many residents had little time to evacuate after the fire exploded in size.
“Honestly, I thought it was like in a movie,” said Ruby Delatorre, who got back from a concert only to find her home in the evacuation zone. She returned Sunday to walk to her home and get a few more possessions.
“I literally want to cry and go back for all my stuff. I just got what I thought I needed,” Delatorre said.
Some who spotted the smoke on Saturday thought it was from another wildfire that has been burning the past two weeks.
Teresa Lenshyn, who is among the evacuees, said being forced to leave was chaotic and stressful. “You don’t know if the winds are going to shift and it’s going to get worse,” she said.
The Hawk Fire is the third large wildfire in the rugged terrain north of Reno in the past two weeks — all caused by human activity, according to fire officials.
Extremely dry conditions across the American West have sparked a rash of wildfires this summer. Fires in eastern Washington state forced the evacuation of 60,000 people in the Spokane area in early August.
Nationwide, there have been more than 50,000 wildfires — the most over the past 10 years covering the same time period, according to the National Interagency Fire Center.
___
Seewer reported from Toledo, Ohio. Associated Press reporters John Raby in Charleston, West Virginia, and Jack Brook in New Orleans contributed.

Thunderstorms in central and northern Italy, yellow alert in eight regions

00:22:37 KST

Thunderstorms are sweeping central
and northern Italy, and a yellow bad-weather alert in two
regions will be extended to six others Tuesday, the civil
protection department said Monday.
                                    
                                       
				A fast-moving Atlantic low-pressure system in the northwest will
bring about a period of severe weather and thunderstorms that,
starting in the Alpine regions, will extend to most of the
remaining central and northern regions tomorrow.
                                    
                                       
				Based on available forecasts, the civil protection department,
in consultation with the affected regions, has issued a severe
weather warning.
                                    
                                       
				Scattered to widespread precipitation is expected this
afternoon/evening, including showers and thunderstorms,
initially in the Aosta Valley, Piedmont, and the autonomous
province of Bolzano, and extending from the early hours of
tomorrow, Tuesday, August 25, to Liguria, Emilia-Romagna,
Tuscany, Umbria, Marche, and Lazio.
                                    
                                       
				These events will be accompanied by heavy showers, strong wind
gusts, localized hailstorms, and frequent electrical activity.
                                    
                                       
				Based on forecast events, a yellow alert has been issued for
tomorrow, Tuesday, August 25, for the risk of thunderstorms and
hydrogeological risks in areas of Piedmont, Lombardy, Liguria,
Emilia-Romagna, and Lazio, as well as the entire territory of
Tuscany, Marche, and Umbria.

'Hindu baiter': CM Himanta slams Rahul Gandhi over Manusmriti row
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
15:22:26 UTC
00:22:26 KST

Assam chief minister Himanta Biswa Sarma slams Rahul Gandhi over Manusmriti comment. Photo credit: ANI
NEW DELHI: Assam chief minister Himanta Biswa Sarma on Monday joined the escalating political row over Rahul Gandhi’s remarks on the Manusmriti, accusing the Congress leader of using women’s rights as a 'smokescreen' to target Hinduism and challenging him to support a Uniform Civil Code (UCC).Sarma, in a post on X, said, “Rahul Gandhi is NO champion of women’s rights. He is simply a Hindu baiter, using the cause of women’s empowerment as a convenient smokescreen to target and discredit Hindu Dharma.”The Assam chief minister’s remarks came after Gandhi, during an interaction with girl students in Pune on Saturday, criticised what he called a “Manusmriti mindset” and urged women to “smash patriarchy”.Sarma questioned Gandhi’s position by asking, “Consider this: how many times has he spoken with the same outrage against patriarchal and medieval practices that persist under Sharia? NEVER.”He further argued that Manusmriti and Sharia should not be treated as equivalent sources of authority, writing, “While Sharia is foundational to Islamic jurisprudence, Manusmriti has no comparable scriptural authority in Hindu Dharma. It is not even a universally binding code for Hindus.”
Sarma then directly challenged the Congress leader over the issue of a common civil law. “So here is a simple challenge to Rahul Gandhi: If women’s equality truly comes before politics, will he unequivocally support a Uniform Civil Code that guarantees equal rights irrespective of religion?” he said.
Sarma also alleged that Congress had a record of compromising on women’s rights, referring to the Shah Bano case and the Triple Talaq legislation. He further claimed that Gandhi had ensured that no woman was included in the Karnataka state Cabinet.
The remarks came after the LoP’s interaction with students, where he said women should not be defined solely through their relationships with men.Rahul said women belong to themselves and not to their fathers, husbands or sons, while describing as shameful the Manusmriti’s prescription that women remain under the authority of male relatives at different stages of their lives.Referring to a verse from the ancient text, Gandhi said, “These words are a shame. These words should not have been spoken.”The controversy has since widened, with Uttar Pradesh chief minister Yogi Adityanath, Maharashtra CM Devendra Fadnavis among other prominent BJP faces criticised Rahul’s remarks.CM Adityanath earlier questioned Gandhi’s role as Leader of Opposition and said, “Whether you accept the Manusmriti or not is your choice, but do not acquaint people with wrong facts.”

Smoke-free 2040 goal failing as young adults keep vaping
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
15:21:29 UTC
00:21:29 KST

Smoke-free 2040 goal failing as young adults keep vaping
See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results
A survey has suggested that four in ten young adults in the Netherlands smoke or vape, pointing to how far the country remains from its goal of a smoke-free generation by 2040.
Under the National Prevention Agreement, the government wants nobody in the younger generation to be smoking or vaping by then, and fewer than 5% of adults to do so.
The 40% figure comes from a poll of 2,000 people by comparison website Independer, which found the habit most common among 18-to-29-year-olds. The share drops to around 30% among people in their thirties, 17% among the over-45s and 10% among the over-60s.
Independer is a commercial comparison site rather than a statistical agency, and its figure is well above official data. National statistics agency CBS put the proportion of 18-to-25-year-olds who smoke or vape at roughly a quarter in 2024.
Doctors want a ban
 Paediatric lung specialist Noor Rikkers, of the Artsen slaan alarm (doctors sound the alarm) campaign group, called the numbers shocking and said the rise of vaping was drawing in ever-younger children.
She wants the government to ban all products that contain nicotine, arguing that a blanket ban would be simpler to enforce than the current patchwork of rules.
Social media a factor
 The survey also pointed to social media. About three-quarters of young adults said they saw videos of people smoking or vaping, and a third of those said the clips tempted them to try it.
More than a quarter of the young smokers who watched such videos said they bought cigarettes or vapes through social media, even though online and social-media sales of tobacco and vapes have been banned since 2023 – a rule dealers have been flouting on Snapchat.
Flavoured vapes have been banned since January 2024, but doctors and inspectors say a black market keeps supplying illegal products.

In Ukraine, stamps titled &quot;We Are Waiting!&quot; were issued for Independence Day in support of prisoners of war
Ukraine
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
18:20:58 EEST
00:20:58 KST

In Ukraine, stamps titled "We Are Waiting!" were issued for Independence Day in support of prisoners of war
Kyiv • UNN
Ukrposhta issued two stamps for the 35th anniversary of Independence in support of prisoners of war and missing persons. They were chosen by nearly 328,800 people.
For the 35th anniversary of Ukraine’s independence, Ukrposhta, together with the Coordination Headquarters for the Treatment of Prisoners of War, issued the "We Are Waiting!" postage stamps as a sign of support for Ukrainian prisoners of war, missing persons, and their families, UNN reports, citing Ukrposhta.
Details
Ukraine is marking its fifth Independence Day during the full-scale war thanks to the people who stood up to defend it. Some of them are still being held captive in Russia, while the fate of others remains unknown. Their relatives, friends, fellow soldiers — and the entire country for whose independence they fought — are waiting for them.
The postage issue consists of two stamps — "Stork. Freedom" by artist Nikita Titov and "Messenger of Good News" by Andriy Yermolenko. Both works are about waiting, hope, and freedom that cannot be taken away.
"Stork. Freedom" depicts a bird with wings in the colors of the Flag of Hope, a symbol of support for prisoners of war and missing persons. White represents faith in their return, while black represents the unknown. The stork, which always returns home, symbolizes the return awaited by thousands of Ukrainian families.
On "Messenger of Good News," a prisoner of war’s hand reaches through the bars toward a tit, a symbol of good news. Even in captivity, Ukrainian soldiers preserve the spirit of freedom. And this postage issue will carry the most important message from home around the world: you are awaited here. In a free and independent Ukraine.
Ukrainians chose both designs during an open vote in the "Diia" app, in which nearly 328.8 thousand people took part.
A ceremony for the special cancellation of the new postage stamps was held at the Main Post Office with the participation of the leadership of Ukrposhta, the Main Intelligence Directorate, the Ministry of Digital Transformation, and families of prisoners of war and those released from Russian captivity. Events were held simultaneously in all regions of Ukraine.
On August 24, an exhibition of portraits of Ukrainian servicemen who returned from Russian captivity also opened at the Main Post Office. It will run for a week. Alongside the postage symbols honoring those who are still awaited, the faces of those who have returned home have appeared.
"Independence Day is a bright holiday. But even on this day — or rather, precisely on this day — we must remember those who have done and continue to do everything to defend our independence. Those who are currently being held captive in Russia, and those considered missing.
For them and their families, every indication that they are remembered, awaited, and that efforts to secure their return continue is extremely important. Starting today, hundreds of thousands of our stamps will spread across Ukraine and the world to remind us again and again: we remember each and every one. And we are waiting for each and every one to come home," said Ihor Smilianskyi, CEO of JSC "Ukrposhta."
The print run of each of the two postage stamps is 360,000 copies. Both have a denomination of U.
A "First Day" envelope designed by Oleksii Kustovskyi, with a print run of 25,000 copies, and a presentation folder with a print run of 500 copies were also created for the issue. The stamps were printed using the offset method.

Indonesia partners with Anant Ambani’s Vantara to strengthen wildlife healthcare and conservation
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
15:20:43 UTC
00:20:43 KST

Indonesia partners with Anant Ambani’s Vantara to strengthen wildlife healthcare and conservation
Aug 24, 2026, 20:50 IST
Comments
Share
AA
Text Size
Small
Medium
Large
Indonesia’s Ministry of Forestry and Vantara, founded by Anant Ambani, Executive Director of Reliance Industries Limited, have announced a partnership focused on strengthening wildlife health infrastructure, veterinary care and conservation efforts in Indonesia.The programme will include training for elephant caregivers, veterinarians and officials involved in wildlife management, along with 10 veterinary hospitals, or ‘Hospitals on Wheels’ for wild animals across Indonesia. The Way Kambas Elephant Hospital will be upgraded into an Elephant Treatment and Care Centre with equipment and technology.
The upgraded facility is expected to support 62 Sumatran elephants under human care and nearly 100 wild Sumatran elephants in the surrounding landscape, according to the announcement.The programme is part of a Memorandum of Understanding between the Indonesian government and Vantara covering wildlife health, animal welfare and conservation. The cooperation also includes conservation breeding, rehabilitation, rewilding, in situ and ex situ conservation, and capacity building.
Vantara will provide veterinary expertise, technical support, infrastructure assistance and training in programmes led by Indonesian authorities, working with national institutions and Fauna Land.
Indonesia’s Forestry Minister Raja Juli Antoni said the cooperation would strengthen the country’s ability to care for wildlife while ensuring that the programmes remain under Indonesian authorities.“Wildlife conservation is ultimately our responsibility, and we must ensure that Indonesia has the facilities, expertise and resources to care for our wildlife wherever they need us. Through this partnership, we are strengthening our own wildlife health and conservation infrastructure, with Vantara supporting us with its experience, expertise and resources,” Antoni said.Vantara CEO Vivaan Karani said the organisation would share its experience in wildlife health, veterinary care, animal husbandry and conservation while also learning from Indonesia’s conservation work.
“Indonesia is home to some of the world's most extraordinary biodiversity, and it is a privilege for Vantara to support the country in protecting this heritage for future generations,” Karani said. “Most importantly, we want this partnership to deliver something practical and lasting for wildlife.”Faunaland CEO Danny Gunalen said the partnership could help strengthen local capacity and wildlife infrastructure.“Indonesia is blessed with extraordinary wildlife and biodiversity, and protecting this natural heritage is a responsibility we all share,
” Gunalen said. “We hope this partnership will build local capacity, strengthen the infrastructure available to wildlife and, most importantly, make a real and lasting difference to the animals and the people dedicated to protecting them.”The cooperation will follow Indonesia’s national conservation priorities and applicable laws and policies, with its success ultimately measured by how effectively the new support reaches animals in need and translates into stronger, lasting protection for endangered species across the country.

Volkswagen unveiled new Tukan pickup, which will replace Saveiro
Ukraine
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
18:20:38 EEST
00:20:38 KST

Volkswagen unveiled new Tukan pickup, which will replace Saveiro
Kyiv • UNN
Volkswagen has introduced the Tukan pickup in Brazil with single- and double-cab configurations. Sales will begin in early 2027, while the Saveiro will be discontinued.
Volkswagen has finally unveiled the all-new Tukan in Brazil without camouflage. Developed to replace the long-running Saveiro, the new pickup was first shown to the public at the Barretos Rodeo festival in the state of São Paulo, Brazil, in single- and double-cab variants. Sales are scheduled to begin in early 2027, reports UNN , citing Motor1.com.
Details
Larger and more modern than the Saveiro, the Tukan will be produced in São José dos Pinhais, Brazil, and will primarily compete with the Fiat Strada. VW has not yet released data on dimensions, payload capacity, or equipment. However, the two cab configurations suggest the availability of both work-oriented versions and better-equipped models intended for family use.
Although it was initially expected to become a direct competitor to the Fiat Toro, the Volkswagen Tukan will be closer in size to the Chevy Montana. Its task is to occupy the niche between compact and midsize pickups while also serving commercial buyers traditionally covered by the Saveiro.
The single-cab body will compete with more affordable versions of the Fiat Strada, offering a larger bed and a greater emphasis on cargo transportation. The double-cab model, meanwhile, is expected to offer turbocharged engines, automatic transmissions, and more extensively equipped configurations, broadening VW's appeal among buyers who also use their pickup as a personal vehicle.
The design follows the visual identity of VW's latest models. At the front, narrow headlights are connected by a horizontal strip, while the bumpers feature pronounced shapes and elements that emphasize the width of the body. At the rear, the taillights feature lighting in the shape of the letter "E" and are connected by a crossbar running across the full width of the tailgate.
VW is still keeping the technical specifications secret, but the Tukan is expected to offer different powertrains depending on the version. Base trims are expected to retain the familiar naturally aspirated 1.6 MSI engine with a manual transmission — a simple and more affordable combination aimed at corporate buyers and small producers.
Mid-level versions may receive a 1.0 TSI flex-fuel engine paired with a six-speed automatic transmission — or may even feature the new eight-speed gearbox recently introduced in the T-Cross. At the top of the range, the pickup is expected to debut in Brazil with the new 1.5 TSI Evo2 engine and a 48-volt mild-hybrid system, a technology that could later be extended to other models of the brand produced at the local plant.
The architecture will be based on the MQB platform, which is already used in the Polo, Nivus, Tera, and T-Cross. However, at the rear, engineers opted for a solid axle with leaf springs — a common construction for trucks that is also used by the competing Fiat Strada. This layout should provide greater payload capacity and durability in commercial use.
The arrival of the Tukan will also mark the end of the Saveiro.
Production is expected to end in the last quarter of 2026, making room in the lineup for the new pickup.

Helium balloon triggers explosions, train disruptions at Ben Gurion | Watch
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
15:19:50 UTC
00:19:50 KST

A helium balloon drifted into the railway station at Ben Gurion International Airport on Monday afternoon, causing electrical cables to short circuit and producing several loud bangs that disrupted train service at the country’s main international airport.
Passengers reported hearing explosions before people on the platform were directed into the airport terminal. Platform 2 was temporarily closed to train traffic following the incident.
Israel Railways described the incident as a localized technical malfunction that was being repaired. Some trains halted their journeys before reaching their final destinations, while others were delayed.
“There was a localized malfunction that is currently being handled. Trains are running,” Israel Railways said. “There may be delays in the near future, but train traffic has not been halted.”
The company disputed reports that the airport railway station had been evacuated, saying it “was not evacuated of passengers and continues normal operations.”
‘There were three explosions’
One passenger who contacted ynet said an electrical cable had been severed and that the 3:09 p.m. train from Jerusalem to Herzliya became stranded several minutes before reaching Ben Gurion Airport.
“There was no electricity or air conditioning for 10 minutes,” the passenger said, adding that passengers were later informed that the train would return to Jerusalem.
Another passenger said the train stopped at the station before three loud explosions were heard. “They have now evacuated everyone from the platform to the terminal,” the passenger said.
Alex, who was aboard one of the trains at the time, told ynet that his train had already arrived at the airport station when he heard several explosions.
“We stopped at the station and then there were several sounds of explosions, and the cable for the train opposite us snapped and fell,” he said. “There were flashes of light from an electrical short circuit and then another explosion. They then announced over the public-address system that we had to evacuate the train and led everyone into Ben Gurion Airport.”
Because of the resulting disruptions, Alex left the station and continued his journey by bus.
A senior Israel Railways official offered a stark assessment of the incident, warning that the railway had repeatedly narrowly avoided a serious accident.
“Every time, only an obvious miracle saves civilians from a deadly disaster,” the official told ynet. “But everyone knows that even obvious miracles run out one day. Once again, they will look for a scapegoat to blame. The disaster will come; only the date is unknown.”
Leadership vacuum at Israel Railways
The disruption comes as Israel Railways, the state-owned company that operates the country’s passenger and freight rail network, has been without its two most senior permanent executives for nearly two months: a board chairman and a CEO.
Moshe Shimoni, who served as chairman for about six years, left the position last month after being appointed chairman of Mekorot, Israel’s national water company.
Meanwhile, nearly a year after CEO Moshe “Shiko” Zana left the railway, a permanent replacement has yet to be appointed. Avner Flor, a former head of the Transportation Ministry’s infrastructure administration, is serving as acting CEO.
Industry officials say the difficulty in filling the CEO position is unsurprising because it is considered one of the most complex management jobs in Israel’s public sector.
Along with overseeing infrastructure projects worth tens of billions of shekels, the railway’s chief executive must contend with a powerful workers union, operational failures that frequently cause service disruptions, safety incidents and accidents, as well as persistent public criticism over the quality and reliability of train service.
Years of management upheaval
Israel Railways has experienced significant management turnover in recent years.
During Shimoni’s tenure as chairman, two CEOs were replaced, while a string of senior executives left the company, including its chief financial officer, legal adviser, head of human resources and head of the railway electrification administration.
More recently, the executive responsible for freight operations was removed from his position following two freight-train accidents.
The company has also faced delays to its electrification program, the end of its contract with Spanish infrastructure company SEMI and operational challenges during the war with Iran.
The upheaval comes at a particularly consequential period for Israel’s railway network, which is preparing to operate and complete some of the largest infrastructure projects in its history.
They include the Eastern Railway, the Route 431 railway, a fourth track through Tel Aviv’s heavily congested Ayalon rail corridor and completion of the nationwide electrification project.
For now, Israel Railways continues to operate under interim leadership at a time when it is expected to carry out its most ambitious development plans while also working to restore public confidence in the reliability and safety of its service.

Experts urge focus on harm, loss of control in tackling gaming addiction
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
15:19:36 UTC
00:19:36 KST

Spending hours in front of a screen does not, by itself, make someone addicted to gaming.
A player can spend long periods gaming, streaming or using social media without losing control or suffering serious consequences, while another person who spends less time online may see school performance decline, relationships deteriorate or psychological distress begin to take over.
That distinction is becoming increasingly important as researchers, parents and policymakers try to determine where intense engagement ends and harmful behavior begins.
For behavioral addiction researcher and psychologist Maeva Flayelle, there is no simple numerical answer.
Speaking about the challenges of identifying problematic gaming behavior, Flayelle said there is no fixed number of hours that separates healthy gaming from addiction.
“There is clearly no consensus on this matter,” she told Anadolu.
“There is no quantitative threshold to determine what is too much related to gaming,” Flayelle added.
She said some people may spend large amounts of time gaming, streaming or using social media without experiencing harmful consequences.
“You can have really highly engaged people in gaming, streaming or social media use, yet they don't encounter any negative consequences or they don't have any loss of control with that,” she said.
At the same time, Flayelle said someone who spends less time gaming may still experience significant problems.
“You can have people who will game a bit less, but who will have significant psychological distress and functional impairment in some domains of their lives,” she said, citing work, personal relationships and social relationships as examples.
“So, this is not a question of hours or amount of hours. This is really a question of outcomes and consequences in people's lives,” she said.
Flayelle said one of the main distinctions between ordinary gaming and problematic behavior is whether a person can continue to control how they engage with games.
“We can draw the line when people start to lose control over the time they spend gaming,” she said.
Repeated unsuccessful efforts to reduce gaming can also indicate a problem, she added.
Flayelle said the situation becomes more concerning when gaming is accompanied by “psychological distress” and negative feelings such as “guilt or regret.”
“Yet, we start to enter this problematic area,” she said.
Flayelle also pointed to the way video games are designed, particularly the use of loot boxes, as an area requiring closer scrutiny.
“We know more and more that loot boxes can cause some issues and cause some prolonged involvement with potential loss of control,” she said.
She said regulatory developments are already taking place and argued that current evidence gives policymakers reason to pay attention to the feature.
“With the evidence that we have today, loot boxes seem to be a design feature for which we should have attention in regulatory terms,” she said.
Young people require particular attention because of their stage of development, according to Flayelle.
“Of course, young people are a vulnerable group,” she said.
“We need to pay attention to them because, in terms of brain maturity, we still need to develop, notably for functions that underlie decision-making and self-regulation,” she added.
Flayelle said parents should not focus solely on screen time but should look more closely at what their children play, how they play and whether gaming is affecting other areas of their lives.
“Parents should monitor which games they play and how they play, and most importantly, they need to monitor whether there are some negative outcomes again in their lives,” she said.
She pointed to social isolation and declining school performance as possible warning signs.
“If you have a young individual who starts to isolate himself in the bedroom, always playing, there are no more social relationships, or the school grades are really decreasing,” she said.
“These are warning signals that parents should pay attention to.”
Flayelle said gaming disorder has received increasing attention from policymakers and regulators following its official recognition by the World Health Organization.
But she said researchers also need greater cooperation from gaming companies.
“What we need most today is probably to be able to enter a dialogue with the industry, with the gaming industry,” she said.
Researchers need access to industry data to improve studies of gaming behavior, she said.
“We would need, as researchers, to access their data to improve the research that we conduct on those behaviors,” Flayelle said.
She added that gaming companies also need to take greater responsibility.
“They also need to acknowledge that they probably should take greater responsibility for the way these video games are designed to prevent harms for users,” she said.
While Flayelle focused on identifying and preventing gaming-related harm, psychiatrist Jon Grant addressed how behavioral addictions can be treated once they become problematic.
Grant said researchers have conducted several double-blind, placebo-controlled trials over approximately 25 years examining treatments for behavioral addictions.
“I think the evidence base is good; it's not great,” Grant said.
Behavioral addictions differ from substance-related disorders because they do not involve consuming an external substance, raising questions about what medication should target.
Grant said treatment may depend on whether a patient is dealing only with the problematic behavior or also has other co-occurring conditions.
“If the person has no other problems, you can target the behavior itself,” he said.
“If they have these other co-occurring problems, that might mean that they may need two different medications or a slightly different medication based on whichever the co-occurring problem is,” he added.
Grant said he would like medication to become part of standard treatment alongside psychological approaches.
“I hope that they become a standard treatment along with psychotherapy,” he said.
“If we believe that these behavioral addictions are serious, then it seems as if we should do everything possible to help people with them.”
For Grant, that means clinicians should not necessarily view medication and psychotherapy as competing treatment options.
“And if that includes a combination of medication and psychotherapy, why wouldn't we do both?” he said.

Israel subjecting Russian historian to brutal interrogation – lawyer
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
15:19:06 UTC
00:19:06 KST

Israel subjecting Russian historian to brutal interrogation – lawyer
Russian-Israeli historian Artyom Kirpichenok has been subjected to brutal treatment while in Shin Bet custody, including interrogations lasting up to 24 hours, sleep deprivation, prolonged handcuffing, and threats, according to his lawyer.
Kirpichenok, 51, disappeared on August 2 shortly after arriving at Ben Gurion Airport from Yerevan. His whereabouts remained unknown for nearly three weeks before his supporters revealed that he was being held at a detention facility in Petah Tikva.
His lawyer, Inna Lebedinskaya-Katz, was finally allowed to meet with him for around an hour on Sunday after repeatedly seeking access, according to Nika Dubrovsky, a Russian-born artist and writer campaigning for his release.
Citing Lebedinskaya-Katz, Dubrovsky said Kirpichenok has faced interrogation sessions lasting 18 to 24 hours while deprived of sleep. He is allegedly being kept handcuffed and hunched over on an iron stool fixed to the floor, with three or four interrogators standing behind him “shouting, threatening, and applying constant pressure.” He has also allegedly been threatened with life imprisonment.
Kirpichenok is in “tolerable physical condition” and has no immediate health complaints, but is “exhausted, tormented and deeply depressed,” Dubrovsky wrote on X on Monday.
”He understands that his friends have not abandoned him. He is holding on and sends his greetings to everyone,” she wrote. Kirpichenok also asked that no one come to Israel.
The allegations have not been independently verified. However, international human rights groups previously documented sleep deprivation and painful restraint during Shin Bet interrogations. Israel’s Supreme Court has ruled against deliberately using prolonged sleep deprivation to break suspects.
Kirpichenok is being held underground with the lights on around the clock and denied books and writing materials, Dubrovsky cited his lawyer as saying. A gag order remains in force, and the defense has yet to receive the case files or details of the accusations.
Kirpichenok, a dual Russian-Israeli citizen and Middle East expert, is known for criticizing Israel’s policies toward Iran and Palestine. Moscow sought information from the Israeli authorities following his disappearance and said it is closely monitoring the case.
Kirpichenok recently worked with media outlets and civic organizations in Türkiye and Iran and frequently traveled to Iran. Israeli citizens are generally prohibited from traveling to countries they are at war with. Dubrovsky, however, said the trips now appear to be of little interest to investigators.
Born in Russia, Kirpichenok moved to Israel in the 1990s, studied at the Hebrew University of Jerusalem and served in the Israel Defense Forces before returning to Russia. He later became a vocal critic of the Israeli government and its military campaign in Gaza, describing Israel as a Western “colonial project” in his recent book ‘Israel: The Road to Catastrophe’.
The Israeli authorities have yet to publicly disclose any charges against him.

Regulation on recognising foreign same-sex marriages comes into force in Poland
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
15:18:49 UTC
00:18:49 KST

Keep our news free from ads and paywalls by making a donation to support our work!
Notes from Poland is run by a small editorial team and is published by an independent, non-profit foundation that is funded through donations from our readers. We cannot do what we do without your support.
All registry offices in Poland can now recognise same-sex marriages conducted in other European Union countries, after a government regulation issued in May went into force. The decision came in response to a ruling by the Court of Justice of the EU (CJEU) requiring Poland to recognise such unions.
Deputy prime minister Krzysztof Gawkowski expressed his “great joy, because finally in Poland we have a law that gives the right to dignity”. The government’s plenipotentiary for equality, Katarzyna Kotula, warned local authorities that they will be breaking the law if they refuse to comply with the new rules.
However, the mayor of one town in a conservative region has already declared that his administration will not recognise such marriages. He argues that doing so would violate Poland’s constitution, which mentions marriage as a union between a man and a woman.
Od jutra polskie państwo będzie lepiej chronić godność i prawa swoich obywateli.
Nowe wzory aktów małżeństwa umożliwią transkrypcję zagranicznych aktów małżeństw jednopłciowych z pełnym szacunkiem dla par. To koniec administracyjnej fikcji i niepewności, z którą przez lata… pic.twitter.com/mRZYkEg9W4
— Krzysztof Gawkowski (@KGawkowski) August 22, 2026
Polish domestic law still does not allow any form of same-sex unions to be formed. However, last year, the CJEU ordered that the country recognise same-sex marriages conducted in other EU member states. That was followed by a similar ruling from Poland’s own Supreme Administrative Court (NSA).
One obstacle to implementing those decisions was that, previously, Poland’s civil registry only allowed male-female marriages to be entered. But, under a regulation issued by the government in May, the system now allows either mixed-sex or same-sex marriages.
Even before that went into force on Sunday, some Polish cities, starting with Warsaw and Wrocław, had already begun transcribing same-sex marriages conducted abroad into their registries. They simply listed one spouse in the “man” section and the other as a “woman”, even though that was not accurate for one of them.
But some local authorities have refused to do so, pointing to article 18 of Poland’s constitution, which states that: “Marriage, being a union of a man and a woman, as well as the family, motherhood and parenthood, shall be placed under the protection and care of the Republic of Poland.”
Such doubts were cited by the mayor of Zakopane, a town in southern Poland, last week when he announced that his administration did not intend to begin recognising foreign same-sex marriages after the regulation came into force.
“We will not be issuing such transcriptions in Zakopane,” said Łukasz Filipowski, quoted by local news service Nasze Miasto. He argued that doing so would “not be in accordance with the constitution”, adding that, while Zakopane is “tolerant”, it “promotes the traditional family model…[as] our foundation”.
Filipowski pointed to a ruling in July by the Constitutional Tribunal (TK), which found that the government’s regulation conflicts with the constitution’s definition of marriage. However, the government considers the TK to be improperly constituted and therefore regards its rulings as invalid.
Poland's Constitutional Tribunal has ruled that a new regulation allowing foreign same-sex marriage certificates to be registered in Poland is unconstitutional.
The government says it will not implement the rulinghttps://t.co/xDAzLjcJQ4
— Notes from Poland 🇵🇱 (@notesfrompoland) July 28, 2026
In response to Filipowski’s remarks, Kotula told broadcaster TVN that officials who refuse to recognise foreign same-sex marriages “will be breaking the law”. She said that the personal views of officials cannot “translate into what [legal] actions they take” and that the government would be monitoring the situation.
Gawkowski – who, like Kotula, is a leading figure in The Left (Lewica), a junior member of Poland’s ruling coalition – also warned municipalities against issuing refusals. He noted that, as well as the CJEU ruling, Polish courts have also issued orders to recognise foreign same-sex marriages.
“In the end, these local authorities will lose [a court battle], so why embarrass themselves? Let’s not look for legal tricks. Let’s just implement something that has been done in other, normal countries for years,” Gawkowski told Polsat News.
However, even once same-sex marriages are transcribed into Polish civil registries, there remains uncertainty about what legal consequences that will have in practice, especially given that Poland’s domestic law does not allow for any legally recognised form of same-sex union.
Legal experts say that it may take years – and potentially many more legal challenges by same-sex couples – for the situation to be clarified.
Separately, parliament earlier this year approved a bill, proposed by the ruling coalition, that would have granted same-sex couples in Poland certain rights normally granted to married couples. However, the law was vetoed by President Karol Nawrocki, who is aligned with the right-wing opposition.
Poland has begun recognising foreign same-sex marriages. But the legal consequences for such couples remain unclear, especially as Polish law does not allow any form of same-sex union
