UK to help Ukraine build Storm Shadow long-range missiles: Why it matters
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
15:17:36 UTC
00:17:36 KST

UK to help Ukraine build Storm Shadow long-range missiles: Why it matters
The UK’s willingness to share classified missile-production knowledge sets a potentially important precedent.
The United Kingdom is set to share classified weapons information with Ukraine to enable it to produce its own version of the Storm Shadow long-range cruise missile, designed jointly with France, as Prime Minister Andy Burnham visits Kyiv on his first foreign trip as the UK’s prime minister.
Burnham’s trip on Monday, timed to coincide with Ukraine’s 35th anniversary of independence from the Soviet Union, is the latest show of support on the part of the UK and Ukraine’s other European allies at a time when backing from the United States has been erratic, analysts said.
Recommended Stories
list of 3 items
- list 1 of 3Russia, Ukraine trade attacks as Putin warns of ‘far more destructive’ hits
- list 2 of 3Putin warns Ukraine has opened ‘Pandora’s box’, rejects peace proposals
- list 3 of 3Zelenskyy says wartime elections could ‘destroy’ Ukraine
Burnham described Russia’s war on Ukraine as “four and a half years of Russia’s full-scale invasion … aimed at crushing the independence we are here to celebrate” as he appeared alongside Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelenskyy at a ceremony in Kyiv.
“But today and every day, you prove that they have failed.”
Burnham is also to co-chair a meeting of the so-called Coalition of the Willing, those nations prepared to provide support for war-torn Ukraine.
The sharing of top-secret weapons blueprints stands in contrast to US President Donald Trump’s backpedalling last month on a promise to allow Ukraine to manufacture its own version of the US-made Patriot air defence interceptor, which Ukraine says it badly needs to counter Russian ballistic missile attacks.
While the US has provided Ukraine with US-made Patriot air defence batteries and munitions in the past, Trump last month said handing over the underlying technology to Ukraine to be able to independently produce them was “a hard thing to give away”.
The UK’s decision to share blueprints, however, follows a similar move by France and comes as Ukraine and Russia exchange heavy air attacks and Russian President Vladimir Putin warns of intensified retaliatory strikes on Ukrainian economic infrastructure.
Ukraine has stepped up its domestic military production, crafting long-range drones and missiles that strike deep into Russian territory, but analysts said producing its own Storm Shadows will give Kyiv the ability to hit Russia’s strategic targets more efficiently and at an unprecedented rate.
What are Storm Shadow long-range missiles?
Also known as SCALP in France, these missiles have a range of about 560km (350 miles) and are capable of hitting targets 240km (150 miles) inside Russian territory. This is not as far as Ukraine’s in-house, deep-strike drones, such as the FP-5 Flamingo cruise missile, which has a range of 3,000km (1,865 miles).
But while Ukrainian long-range missiles can hit “soft” targets inside Russia, such as Wildberries warehouses, Storm Shadow missiles give Kyiv the ability to hit “hard” targets, including heavily fortified, guarded locations that are usually protected by much tougher defences.
Their advanced navigation systems also mean they can be used at night and in all weather conditions.
Last year, the Ukrainian military’s General Staff said Storm Shadow missiles made by the manufacturer MBDA and provided by the UK had been used to hit Russian facilities, including a chemical plant and the Novoshakhtinsk oil refinery, one of the biggest oil product suppliers in southern Russia. This year, they were used to attack Russia’s Black Sea naval headquarters in the port city of Sevastopol in Crimea.
One Storm Shadow missile is estimated to cost about $1m to produce, so they are deployed in more carefully calibrated attacks than the flurry of cheap drones that Ukraine already mass-produces.
How easy will it be for Ukraine to build its own Storm Shadow missiles?
Keir Giles, an associate fellow at the Chatham House think tank, said Ukraine is likely to have a good capacity for producing these more sophisticated weapon systems once it has the blueprints.
“Ukraine, under the force of fighting a war of national survival, has been developing a very agile, highly motivated and distributed defence industry, which is far ahead in terms of adaptation to current needs [compared with] some of its Western European counterparts, including the UK,” he told Al Jazeera.
Being able to produce higher quantities of this missile, more than its actual capability, is what could make the difference, he added.
“Ukraine has had to be highly selective in the targets against which it uses its very limited stocks of the most advanced weapons systems,” Giles said. “Russia, meanwhile, has been exponentially increasing its production of drones and missiles, a trend that’s been in place for several years now.”
“So redressing that balance would be a major step forward for Ukraine, especially if it is able to reduce the number of missiles being launched at Ukraine every night by striking at the facilities that enable those launches,” the analyst added.
Has Ukraine been able to produce any of its own long-range missiles?
Yes. Besides its mass production of cheap short-range drones and interceptors, Ukraine is capable of designing and manufacturing more sophisticated longer-range missile systems, including the Neptune and Flamingo cruise missiles.
Karan Vassil, a Eurasia analyst at the UK-based private intelligence firm Sibylline, said Ukraine has also been developing and testing its own ballistic missile systems.
However, the Storm Shadow/SCALP “is a highly sophisticated weapons system incorporating decades of Anglo-French research and development”, Vassil told Al Jazeera. “Licensed production gives Ukraine access to knowledge and industrial processes that would otherwise take considerable time and resources to reproduce.”
Giles, who also leads the Conflict Studies Research Centre, a group of experts in Eurasian security, said Ukraine may well already have knowledge of the design of Storm Shadow missiles but using it without cooperation would be a “slap in the face to its coalition supporters”.
The reason Ukraine is only now getting access to the missiles’ blueprints has to do with politics, he said. “The United States is not willing to supply the interceptors for Patriot missile systems, so there’s a gap in Ukraine’s air and missile defences, which Russia has been exploiting.”
He added: “European partners are taking up the task of making sure that Ukraine can prevail in the war, and a major part of that is ensuring that it can strike into Russia.”
Vassil said British and French willingness to transfer classified missile-production knowledge sets a potentially important precedent for future allied cooperation. “But it remains uncertain whether this will have an impact on Washington’s posture regarding Patriot interceptors,” he said.
How fast can Ukraine scale up production of Storm Shadow missiles?
France and the UK have expressed hope that they can help Ukraine set up a production line by the end of the year.
However, estimates suggested a new batch of cruise missiles of this complexity could take about two years from contract award to delivery, according to Vassil, who added that establishing production will be a challenging endeavour and will most likely continue to depend on a complex international supply chain.
Storm Shadows also incorporate some sensitive US technologies, meaning some aspects of supply or transfer may still require US approval and “it remains uncertain how this will apply to Ukrainian production”, he said.
There are also substantial security challenges. “Missile-production facilities inside Ukraine would present valuable targets for Russia and would have to operate under the continuing threat of Russian long-range missile and drone attacks,” Vassil continued.
“Protecting production sites, dispersing manufacturing and maintaining supplies of critical components could, therefore, become almost as important as obtaining the technical information itself.”

Zelenskyy denies that Ukraine's Black Sea corridor is completely blocked

Quote: "There is no total blockade today; there is an attempt at a total blockade, but three or four ships a day enter and leave the Greater Odesa, despite the fact that they [the Russians – ed.] are striking port infrastructure". [Greater Odesa is a logistical and industrial hub in Odesa Oblast that combines three major ports – Odesa, Chornomorsk and Pivdennyi – ed.]

Is UK's Storm Shadow missile a game changer for Ukraine?

The announcement came during Andy Burnham's first visit to Kyiv as prime minster, where he met Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelensky.

Finland, Ukraine to expand drone cooperation, President says
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
15:12:28 UTC
00:12:28 KST

Şeyma Erkul Dayanç
24 August 2026•Update: 24 August 2026
Finland and Ukraine are expected to reach an agreement on drone cooperation soon, with Finland planning to send 90% of its drone production to Ukraine, Finnish President Alexander Stubb said Monday.
“I think 90% of [Finland's] drone production will go to Ukraine, while 10% of production will remain in Finland,” Stubb said alongside Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelenskyy during a visit to mark Ukraine's Independence Day.
Stubb said the planned agreement would strengthen defense cooperation between the two countries.
He described the planned cooperation as mutually beneficial and compared it with Denmark's model of directly financing Ukrainian defense industry firms.
Stubb said defense cooperation between Finland and Ukraine covers five areas: maritime, air, land, cyber and space.
His delegation to Kyiv included representatives of seven Finnish defense companies. Patria, Insta and Boomeranger signed memorandums of understanding with Ukrainian companies on joint production.
Stubb said international support for Ukraine remained strong and expressed confidence that Ukraine would ultimately prevail in its war with Russia.
The Ukrainian capital is hosting a Coalition of the Willing meeting, bringing together countries supporting Ukraine, including the UK and France.

MOSCOW, August 24. /TASS/. Former US president’s special envoy on Ukraine, Keith Kellogg, has spoken out in favor of mobilizing women to serve with the armed forces of Ukraine.
"My daughter was a soldier. She graduated from the US Military Academy and fought in Afghanistan. Do I believe that women should be mobilized into the army? My answer is yes. Because they fight no worse than men," he told Ukraine’s Naspravdi YouTube channel.
"It is very important that women, as part of society, on into service. On both a personal and professional level, I don’t see a problem," Kellogg added.
General mobilization was declared in Ukraine in February 2022 and has been repeatedly extended. Initially, men aged 27 to 60 were subject to conscription, but the mobilization age was lowered to 25 in April 2024. A law tightening mobilization took effect in the country on May 18 of the same year. Men have tried various ways to avoid conscription or leave the country, often risking their lives. Military enlistment office personnel regularly conduct raids and use force against civilians. But even these measures have failed to address the Ukrainian forces’ personnel shortage. Cases of desertion and unauthorized absence from military units continue to rise. The topics of mobilizing women and lowering the mobilization age further are periodically raised in the country.

Ukraine returns 10 servicemen its own records had marked missing in action
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
15:10:40 UTC
00:10:40 KST

Ukraine brought a small group of servicemen home from captivity on 24 August, its Independence Day, and every one of them had been carried as missing in action, Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelenskyy announced. The men crossed back through Belarus as a separate stage of the exchange now under way. Ukraine's prisoner-of-war agency later set out who they were.
Amid Russia's all-out war in Ukraine, prisoner swaps remain one of the few Ukrainian-Russian mechanisms still producing results while every negotiating track on territory and ceasefires stays deadlocked, which leaves each completed stage as the practical measure of what still passes between two governments that agree on nothing else.
Ten men Ukraine had listed as missing, not captured
President Zelenskyy said Ukraine was bringing back 10 of its soldiers through Belarus. They serve in the Armed Forces and the National Guard, and they defended the country in the Donetsk sector in the east. He called it a special stage of the exchange.
The Coordination Headquarters for the Treatment of Prisoners of War, the state body that runs Ukraine's exchanges, specified the details in the afternoon. The freed men hold the ranks of sergeant, soldier, and sailor. All 10 had been logged as missing rather than captured.
Two had been listed as missing since 2024, the other eight since 2025. The agency said the handover came out of talks run by its own exchange working group. It described the return as the second stage of the current exchange.
The first stage ran on 19 August and brought back 103 servicemen.
Locating Ukrainian captives inside Russia or the occupied territories is often difficult, as is verifying their identities, because Russia withholds data on captured soldiers and civilian hostages. Former detainees describe beatings, electric shocks, mock executions, and refusals of medical care, and civilians held without trial report the same treatment as the soldiers.
Roughly 7,000 Ukrainian service members were in Russian captivity as of May 2026, with civilian detainees estimated at 10,000 to 20,000. Ukraine had returned 9,606 military personnel and civilians through exchanges by late June.

Empire State Building climbers kiss outside court after viral proposal

Empire State Building climbers kiss outside court after viral proposal
New York – A couple who climbed to the top of the Empire State Building and got engaged appeared in a New York court on Aug 24, before giving each other a lingering kiss in full view of the world’s media.
Ivan Kuznetsov, 32, and Angelina Nikolau, 33, face several charges after their viral stunt on July 1 when they clambered up an antenna at the peak of the 443m landmark.
During the climb, the pair, clad in black outfits with their faces covered, unfurled a banner that read: “When the power of love beats the love of power, the world knows peace.”
On the top of the building, Kuznetsov went down on one knee and appeared to propose to Nikolau, before they kissed and embraced as news helicopters swirled around them.
At the brief hearing on Aug 24, the pair each wore black leather jackets with jeans and stood quietly holding hands behind their lawyers.
Outside the courthouse, they embraced one another with a deep, 10-second kiss in front of a pack of journalists and onlookers.
The couple have built a huge online following for their illegal climbing exploits and starred in the Netflix documentary Skywalkers: A Love Story, which showed them scaling Malaysia’s Merdeka 118 – the world’s second tallest building.
Facing US charges including reckless endangerment, burglary and criminal mischief, they have defended their latest climb as “art” in a series of Instagram posts.
“Whatever happens in court, we will respect the process and accept the consequences of our choices,” the pair, who have not entered a plea, wrote online.
A further hearing was scheduled for Sept 29.
The Empire State Building has an observation deck open to the public that offers stunning views of New York, but access to higher points is forbidden. AFP

Ukraine Torches Three Ozon Mega-Warehouses, Hits MiG-29, as Drone Forces Hit 66 Targets in One Night
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
15:09:29 UTC
00:09:29 KST

- Category
- War in Ukraine
Ukraine Torches Three Ozon Mega-Warehouses, Hits MiG-29, as Drone Forces Hit 66 Targets in One Night
Ukrainian strike drones hit three major Ozon logistics centers across southern Russia, igniting large fires at facilities with a combined warehouse area of more than 314,000 square meters, according to photos and videos published by the OSINT community Exilenova+ on August 24.
The strikes formed part of a much broader Ukrainian operation deep behind Russian lines. Robert “Madyar” Brovdi, commander of Ukraine’s Unmanned Systems Forces, said his units successfully engaged 66 Russian targets during the night, including a MiG-29 fighter jet, four radar systems, military facilities and vessels linked to Russia’s shadow fleet.
We bring you stories from the ground. Your support keeps our team in the field.
“Birds of the Unmanned Systems Forces successfully struck 66 enemy targets in the operational depth of the enemy rear on the night of August 24,” Brovdi said.
Among the most significant targets, according to the commander, was another Russian MiG-29 near Dolotinka in Russia’s Rostov region.
“Among the sensitive targets was another MiG-29 [Dolotinka, Rostov region, Russia] and four radar systems: two Kasta-2E2 units, a Nebo-SV radar [all in Russia’s Rostov region] and an ST-68 radar [Rozdolne, Crimea],” Brovdi said.
He also reported the destruction of a base used by Russia’s SOBR special police forces in occupied Strilkove in Ukraine’s Kherson region, as well as strikes against energy infrastructure across Russian-occupied Ukrainian territory.
Two vessels associated with Russia’s shadow fleet—a tanker and a bulk carrier—were also hit in the Black Sea, Brovdi said. Eleven Russian temporary deployment points were struck across occupied parts of the Zaporizhzhia, Donetsk and Luhansk regions.
Three Ozon logistics centers were among the most visible targets of the overnight campaign.
The largest was a warehouse complex near Tyube in Russia’s Dagestan region, covering approximately 130,000 square meters. The site serves as a major logistics hub for northern Dagestan and lies roughly 800 to 900 kilometers from Ukraine’s state border.
A major fire erupted following the strike, with local residents reporting that the glow from the blaze could be seen from as far as 10 kilometers away.
Another Ozon logistics center was targeted in Nevinnomyssk, in Russia’s Stavropol region. The facility covers approximately 93,514 square meters.
The third site was an Ozon warehouse near Enem in Adygea, close to the border with Russia’s Krasnodar region. The complex covers about 91,200 square meters. A large fire developed at the facility after the attack.
Ozon is one of Russia’s largest online marketplaces and operates a sprawling logistics network comparable in function to major Western e-commerce platforms.
An investigation earlier found that Russian troops fighting in Ukraine had repeatedly ordered body armor, thermal-imaging equipment, radios, drone batteries, signal boosters, FPV components and payload-release systems through Ozon. Drone-related products were ordered through Ozon particularly frequently.
Ukraine has already put seven of the ten largest logistics centers operated by Russian e-commerce company Wildberries, OZON rival, out of operation.
Wildberries facilities in Domodedovo and at the Koledino industrial park were among the sites affected. The Koledino facility, covering approximately 250,000 square meters, was the company’s largest warehouse by area.

Syria struggling to deal with attacks on security forces
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
15:07:39 UTC
00:07:39 KST

Breadcrumb
Syria struggling to deal with attacks on security forces
Although the intensity of military confrontations has declined across most of Syria, attacks targeting security and military forces continue to occur, following a pattern of smaller, asymmetric operations. These range from bus bombings and improvised explosive devices (IEDs) to individual assassinations and ambushes.
Available information indicates that a large proportion of these attacks are linked to cells affiliated with Islamic State group. Responsibility for other attacks remains unknown, amid speculation about involvement of groups linked to remnants of the Assad regime.
Since Assad’s ouster in December 2024, ISIS has called on its members to fight the new government led by President Ahmad al-Sharaa, which has joined the US-led international coalition against the organisation.
Overnight buses targeted
Incidents recorded over the past year reveal a clear focus on buses transporting security and military personnel, as they are targets that bring together large numbers of personnel at a specific time and place.
The most recent such attack occurred on Saturday, when a bus belonging to the Internal Security Forces hit an IED on a road in the Damascus countryside, injuring several security personnel.
This came two weeks after a passenger bus was bombed in Jaramana, near Damascus, killing a woman and injuring 13 people.
A similar incident occurred in northeastern Syria in May, when two soldiers were killed in an attack on a military transport bus.
The most high-profile attack came during Emmanuel Macron's visit to the country in July, when Damascus was hit by twin blasts close to the French president's hotel. The interior ministry blamed ISIS which it said had planted explosives in a vehicle and a rubbish container.
Rashid Hourani, a researcher at the Jusoor Centre for Studies, told The New Arab that the targeting of vehicles belonging to the interior and defence ministries reveals weaknesses in security procedures. He also pointed to the sectarian nature of some of the attacks, highlighting the targeting of areas with high Alawite and Christian populations.
A security source who requested anonymity told The New Arab that most bombings and attacks have increasingly focused on overnight buses because they are relatively easy targets. They also believe that requiring personnel to surrender their weapons while on leave may also have increased their vulnerability to attack.
The source suggests providing personnel with weapons or security protection while they are traveling, avoiding the wearing of military uniforms while on leave, and changing travel routes and schedules.
He warned that some attacks may be random and primarily intended to create confusion and embarrass the government.
Assassinations, ambushes
Security forces have also come under fire from assailants in areas of eastern and northern Syria in recent months.
The latest such incidents came on Friday when a soldier and a member of the security forces were killed in separate attacks in Hama and Aleppo.
In recent months, there have also been attacks targeting personnel while they were traveling or outside their duty stations. In May, an attack on a defence ministry facility in Damascus killed one soldier and injured 23 people. Meanwhile, ISIS claimed responsibility for an attack in Hasakah that killed two soldiers.
ISIS also claimed responsibility for detonating a sticky bomb targeting the car of the head of the Justice Department in Babila, Salah Ahmad al-Saleh, seriously injuring him.
The extremist group has intensified its attacks against government forces since the beginning of the year. 29 people, including 23 military personnel, were killed in more than 30 ISIS attacks between 17 February and 19 March. The attacks were concentrated primarily in Deir ez-Zour, Raqqa, and Aleppo, with other incidents recorded in Idlib, Hasakah, and Homs.
Shifting threats
In response, the Syrian authorities have intensified their campaigns against ISIS cells. The Interior Ministry announced earlier this month that it had arrested a senior military commander the Manbij countryside. Last July, the authorities announced operations in Aleppo and Latakia that resulted in the arrest of ISIS members and the seizure of weapons.
The ministry said in June that over a three-month period it had arrested 235 people on terrorism charges and thwarted seven attacks that it said ISIS had been planning.
Military experts partly attribute this to the sensitivity of the transitional period and the presence of operational gaps resulting from the process of integrating the Syrian Democratic Forces (SDF) and other local groups into the army and security forces.
The record of the past year reveals that armed groups have shifted towards trying to wear down government forces through low-level bombings, assassinations, and ambushes.
The most prominent vulnerabilities include the predictable routes and schedules of overnight buses, personnel being exposed while on leave, and the difficulty of controlling open terrain and remote areas.
Major Khaled Abdullah, director of the Interior Minister’s office, said in press statements that the defence and interior ministries have issued directives prohibiting the wearing of official uniforms outside working hours and strengthening security measures. He noted that security and military institutions are working on other solutions to reduce risks.

US to double tariffs on Canadian vehicles after trade talks break down, Trump says
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
15:04:14 UTC
00:04:14 KST

"Canada has been ripping off the United States of America for years," the president wrote in a post on his Truth Social account.
US President Donald Trump announced on Monday that the US would double tariffs on Canadian vehicles from 2027 as he accused Ottawa of "ripping off the United States of America for years."
In a post on his Truth Social account, Trump wrote: "On January First, 2027, Tariffs on all Cars, Trucks, both large and small, Automotive Parts, and Steel, will be increased to 50%."
Current US tariff rates on automobiles stand at 25% for non-US content.
The threats come amid a growing trade war between the two neighbours that hit a flash point on Friday after talks over a deal to avoid 50% duties on some Canadian goods collapsed as both sides accused each other of trying to implement last-minute changes.
Canadian Prime Minister Mark Carney said Canada had been forced to walk away from the "bad" deal as "we cannot accept what they've offered, and we will not give what they've asked."
The levies, which came into effect on Saturday, impact around $20 billion worth of Canadian goods.
Carney warned that Ottawa would match the US tariffs "dollar for dollar to protect our workers and businesses."
"Our response will be concentrated in sectors such as steel, dairy, appliances, agricultural equipment, pulp and paper, and electronics," Carney added in a speech on Saturday.
The Office of the United States Trade Representative described the breakdown in talks as a "missed opportunity" for Canada, which it said was maintaining a "prolonged retaliation" against the US.
"Despite the U.S. offer to Canada to receive the best treatment of any major exporter to our market, new demands and walk backs of other commitments by Canada have upended the careful balance reached in the past days," it said.
Trump's tariff strategy
On Monday, Trump also lashed out at what he called Canada's "ridiculously high tariffs" on US farmers and said the country would no longer be treated "like a state."
Trump has repeatedly suggested that Canada should become the US' "51st state" and said last week that Ottawa wanted "the benefits of being a State, without being one."
The American president has pursued an aggressive tariff policy during his second stint in the White House, raising levies on partners across the world. The administration argues that tariffs will boost US manufacturing and encourage domestic job growth while addressing what it sees as unfair trade rates.
Critics, however, say increasing duties are making life more expensive for American consumers and businesses while fostering widespread uncertainty among companies planning investments and supply chains.

Why Israeli settler attacks on Palestinians in West Bank are pogroms
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
15:02:34 UTC
00:02:34 KST

Why Israeli settler attacks on Palestinians in West Bank are pogroms
Waves of settler attacks, aided by Israel’s military, in the occupied West Bank increasingly fit the definition of a pogrom.
Attacks on Palestinian communities by Israeli settlers in the occupied West Bank, which are often supported by Israeli forces, have gathered pace over the past month since settlers from the Havat Gilad settlement attacked Palestinian homes on the eastern edge of Tal, southwest of Nablus.
On Sunday and Monday, the Israeli military raided multiple towns, villages and refugee camps across the occupied territory, and reports of arrests, home searches and settler attacks have continued to mount.
A 15-day siege on three families comprising 15 people, including two children, in the village of Qusra, which is ongoing, drew a rare public condemnation this month from the United States ambassador to Israel, Mike Huckabee, a staunch supporter of Israel and its West Bank settlements.
Israel is also pushing ahead with plans for the so-called E1 settlement expansion close to Jerusalem, which human rights organisations warn would essentially sever the West Bank in two, further hampering the freedom of movement of Palestinians on either side of it and endangering the possibility of a Palestinian state.
The West Bank attacks increasingly fit the definition of a pogrom, experts said. United Nations Special Rapporteur on the Occupied Palestinian Territories Francesca Albanese described these Israeli attacks against Palestinians as “pogroms against defenceless civilians”.
So why are experts calling settler attacks pogroms, and do they fit the formal definition of one?
What’s happening in the West Bank?
Settlers, frequently supported by the Israeli military, have been attacking Palestinian homes, businesses and farmland across the West Bank. Such attacks have been commonplace for decades as settlements have expanded across the territory, but they have gathered pace after October 7, 2023, the day that Hamas-led fighters attacked southern Israel and Israel launched its genocidal war on Gaza. The attacks in the West Bank have become particularly violent and frequent over the past month.
Israeli settlements and outposts across the West Bank are illegal under international law.
According to the Palestinian Colonization and Wall Resistance Commission, Israeli settlers now number roughly 780,000 across the West Bank and occupied East Jerusalem. They live in 192 settlements and 400 outposts, making up 71 percent of Area C in the West Bank, which is under full Israeli civil and military control, and 91 percent of the Jordan Valley, the commission said.
Settlers often set up outposts without formal approval around Palestinian villages from which they launch a campaign of harassment, including killing and stealing livestock; cutting off water, electricity and supplies; and damaging property to drive local residents out. Once they establish a more formal settlement, they often receive retrospective approval from the Israeli government for the settlement.
In some places more recently, settlers have laid siege to individual family homes, such as in the Ras al-Ein area of Qusra, cutting off water and other supplies and damaging orchards or harming livestock.
This has set a new pattern: Settlers establish a tent adjacent to individual homes; Palestinians’ homes are besieged with essentials cut off; and Israeli soldiers arrive, not to remove the settlers but rather to remove the family – through arrests or for their “protection”.
In Qusra, the three besieged families were forced together into one of the family houses. Meanwhile, Israeli forces have occupied one of the other homes for use as a military post and thrown furniture and belongings from its windows, according to the Palestinian news agency Wafa.
The UN Office for the Coordination of Humanitarian Affairs (OCHA) recorded more than 1,330 settler-related attacks on Palestinians in the West Bank from January to late July, averaging six a day and marking the fastest pace since the agency began systematic tracking two decades ago.
Separately, a UN-appointed commission of inquiry has found that the Israeli authorities have repeatedly failed to prevent foreseeable settler attacks and, in some cases, have directly enabled them through army protection and the provision of weapons and funding.
OCHA has found that settler attacks, demolitions and evictions forcibly displaced more Palestinians in the first four months of this year than in the whole of 2025.
OCHA reported that 107 communities, comprising 5,900 Palestinians, had been completely or partially displaced as a result of settler violence since January 2023. It also found that the Israeli military has expelled another 32,000 Palestinians from their homes in the West Bank since January 2025, essentially emptying the Jenin, Tulkarem and Nur Shams refugee camps.
What is the definition of a pogrom?
Major dictionaries in the US and United Kingdom vary to some degree on the definition.
The British Collins Dictionary identifies a pogrom as “organised, official violence against a group of people for racial or religious reasons” with a notable inclusion of official, or state-backed, violence.
The Oxford Advanced Learner’s Dictionary calls it “the organised killing of large numbers of people, because of their race or religion” without mention of state-sanctioned violence.
Merriam-Webster says it is “an organised massacre of helpless people” – “specifically: such as a massacre of Jews”.
Cambridge Dictionary says it is “an act of organised cruel behaviour or killing that is done to a large group of people because of their race or religion”.
The word is understood to have been introduced via Yiddish and was at first used to describe the wave of violent, organised mob attacks on Jewish communities in the Russian Empire after the assassination of Tsar Alexander II in 1881.
It is important to note that unlike legal terms, including “crimes against humanity” or “genocide”, “pogrom” has no established legal definition under international law.
Can attacks in the West Bank be referred to as pogroms?
Besides UN Special Rapporteur Albanese, who has described Israeli attacks against Palestinians in the West Bank as “pogroms against defenceless civilians”, Israeli officials themselves have described such attacks as “pogroms”.
On February 26, 2023, hundreds of Israeli settlers attacked Huwara and surrounding Palestinian communities after two Israeli settlers were killed. A Palestinian was killed, hundreds were injured, and homes, businesses and cars were burned.
The Israeli military’s then-commander in the West Bank, Yehuda Fuchs, called the settler attacks a “pogrom”, and it was also recorded as such by the US Department of State.
The Israeli-Palestinian human rights group B’Tselem has also previously described Israeli actions in the West Bank as “pogroms”, especially after the February 2023 attacks. Later, it followed up with a report titled, The Pogroms Are Working – the Transfer Is Already Happening, which argued that settler attacks were contributing to the displacement of Palestinian communities.
“Settler violence is not a new phenomenon, but over the past three years, it has become more violent, more systematic and, most importantly, part of a state mechanism intended to ethnically cleanse large parts of the West Bank,” said Yair Dvir, the spokesperson for B’Tselem.
“The government funds and arms the settlers while the Israeli military stands by and, in many cases, actively participates in the attacks,” he told Al Jazeera. “So far, these militias have expelled 64 Palestinian communities and taken control of around 1 million dunams [100,000 hectares/247,100 acres] of land.”
Dvir also told Al Jazeera that the Israeli settlers are “now carrying out organised attacks on established Palestinian villages and towns in order to drive out their residents”.
“These are armed militias operating as an unofficial arm of the state with a shared goal: to ethnically cleanse as much of the West Bank as possible,” he added.
The Lemkin Institute for Genocide Prevention – which is named after Polish Jewish lawyer Raphael Lemkin, who is credited with coining the term genocide after the Holocaust – referred to settler attacks on al-Mughayyir, a Palestinian village in the Ramallah and el-Bireh governorate, as “pogroms”, reporting that hundreds of armed settlers attacked residents and burned buildings and cars while soldiers stood by this year. In 2025, the Israeli military uprooted thousands of Palestinian olive trees in al-Mughayyir.
Raheel Dhattiwala, an Indian scholar who focuses on collective violence, told Al Jazeera: “West Bank attacks fit the characteristics of a pogrom – systematic targeting of one civilian group by another with state approval. A few senior Israeli army officers have called it ‘pogroms’ in the past too, for example after attacks in Huwara.
“One might want to debate the use of the term ‘pogrom’, but it is accurate to call settler violence systematic attacks of occupiers with government approval on groups of occupied people.”

Dome installation begins at Shams Tabrizi mausoleum in Khoy
TEHRAN – The installation of the dome at the mausoleum of Shams Tabrizi in Khoy, a major architectural project in northwestern Iran, has begun, according to the director general of West Azarbaijan’s Cultural Heritage, Tourism and Handicrafts Department.
Morteza Safari said on Monday that the project’s distinctive engineering and architectural features would make the dome a prominent urban landmark in Khoy.
The dome will have a total height of 15.5 meters, placing it among the tallest domes built in northwestern Iran, Safari said.
He added that the first phase of the project, involving construction of the concrete structure, had been completed, while work was continuing on a 9.5-meter-high steel framework.
The dome has been designed as a double-shell structure. Its exterior will feature traditional brickwork, while the interior will be decorated with plasterwork and karbandi, a traditional Iranian architectural technique involving geometric ornamental vaulting.
According to Safari, the brickwork has been inspired by historical periods and incorporates innovative, project-specific techniques intended to distinguish the structure from similar architectural projects.
The dome is expected to serve not only as an important cultural and memorial structure but also as an example of contemporary architectural and engineering achievement in northwestern Iran, he said.
Shams Tabrizi (1185–1248) was a 13th-century Persian mystic whose teachings profoundly influenced Jalal al-Din Rumi. He is the namesake of Rumi’s Divan-e Shams-e Tabrizi and is traditionally associated with Rumi’s spiritual transformation. Khoy, in West Azarbaijan province, has historically served as a crossroads of culture and commerce.

The IDF carried out strikes in the Shati refugee camp deep inside the Gaza Strip on Monday, targeting Hamas infrastructure and weapons-storage facilities, amid growing Israeli concern over the reappearance of kites launched from Gaza toward communities near the border.
The targets were located beyond the “yellow line,” which marks the boundary of the area where Israeli forces are currently deployed inside Gaza.
Ahead of the strikes, evacuation warnings were delivered by telephone to several sites in the area, including an elementary school operated by UNRWA, the UN agency for Palestinian refugees, a medical center run by Doctors Without Borders and a compound belonging to the International Rescue Committee.
Palestinians reported that a mosque and a water desalination facility were among the sites struck. There was no immediate information on casualties.
Kites revive memories of prewar threat
At least five kites have been found in recent days in Kibbutz Kfar Aza and Kibbutz Nahal Oz, Israeli communities near the Gaza border. None was carrying incendiary material or explosives.
Authorities believe the kites were launched from the northern Gaza Strip. They carried various messages written in Arabic, including one declaring, “We will never calm down,” in what Israeli officials viewed as an attempt at psychological terror.
Preliminary IDF assessments indicate that children and teenagers launched the kites. Israeli officials say they did so under instructions from Hamas commanders.
The incidents have revived memories among some residents of southern Israel of the years before the current war, when incendiary kites and balloons launched from Gaza initially appeared to pose a limited threat but eventually became a persistent problem, igniting widespread fires in Israeli agricultural fields and nature reserves near the border.
Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu and Defense Minister Israel Katz held a security assessment Sunday to discuss the issue.
“Israel warns Hamas that if the launches toward Israel do not stop immediately, the IDF will step up targeted operations against those responsible for the launches and evacuate populations from areas from which kites, drones and balloons are launched,” a statement issued after the meeting said.
“Israel will not allow the Hamas terrorist organization or any other element in the Gaza Strip to once again threaten our communities and undermine our citizens’ sense of security,” it added.
Israel presses Hamas to disarm
The developments come as the second phase of a plan for Gaza’s reconstruction has yet to formally begin and Israel continues to insist that Hamas disarm.
Southern Command continues to carry out targeted operations inside Gaza, though on a more limited and focused basis. The military says threats to troops along the yellow line are neutralized when detected.
Under current IDF policy, people identified as having participated in the Hamas-led Oct. 7, 2023, attack on southern Israel can be targeted without requiring additional special authorization, according to Israeli officials.
The military said two Hamas terrorists were killed overnight in the Deir al-Balah area of central Gaza under that policy.

UK approves transfer of Storm Shadow production technology to Ukraine

2026-08-24
18:02:00 EEST
00:02:00 KST

UK approves transfer of Storm Shadow production technology to Ukraine
The UK has approved the transfer of technology for producing Storm Shadow/SCALP cruise missiles to Ukraine.
Source: UK Prime Minister Andy Burnham during a meeting of the Coalition of the Willing in Kyiv on 24 August
Details: According to Burnham, the UK was one of the first countries to provide Ukraine with long-range weapons in 2023.
He said President Volodymyr Zelenskyy has requested access to the UK's Storm Shadow technology so that Ukraine could develop the capability independently. He confirmed that he has approved the request and that British and French defence companies will now work with Ukraine to develop the technology.
The missile is manufactured by European defence company MBDA. According to the company, SCALP – known as Storm Shadow in the UK – is a long-range air-launched air-to-surface cruise missile designed to precisely strike fixed, heavily protected targets, including hardened bunkers and key infrastructure.
According to MBDA, the missile can be used day or night and in all weather conditions. Its guidance system combines inertial navigation, GPS and terrain-referenced navigation. After launch, the missile flies at low altitude to avoid detection. As it approaches its target, an onboard infrared seeker compares the target with a pre-loaded reference image, enabling a high degree of accuracy.
Background:
- Burnham arrived in Kyiv on 24 August on his first overseas visit since taking office. He was expected to announce the handover to Kyiv of secret data on British components for the long-range Storm Shadow/SCALP missile "as Ukraine and France look to establish local assembly lines in Ukraine".
- The decision also complements the UK's Project Brakestop, aimed at rapidly developing low-cost long-range strike capabilities for Ukraine.
- In June, Mykhailo Fedorov, the then Ukrainian defence minister, said that Kyiv was negotiating with France to obtain a licence to manufacture SCALP cruise missiles.

Kerman’s Pateh has potential to become global brand: official
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
15:01:59 UTC
00:01:59 KST

Kerman’s Pateh has potential to become global brand: official
TEHRAN – Pateh, a traditional embroidered handicraft deeply rooted in the history and cultural identity of Iran’s Kerman province, has the potential to become a recognized global brand if commercialization, branding and market development are strengthened, a provincial handicrafts official said.
“Pateh is not merely a handicraft product, but a rooted art that is intertwined with the history and culture of Kerman and reflects an important part of the province’s cultural and artistic heritage,” Kerman’s deputy handicrafts chief Gholamreza Farrokhi said.
The official added that the craft has been passed down through generations within families and local communities, with this continuity playing an important role in preserving its authenticity and ensuring its cultural reproduction.
Pateh is a hand-stitched art form largely based on the skills and creativity of women in Kerman. Farrokhi said supporting the craft could help generate income for families, strengthen the local economy and expand home-based employment.
He stressed the need to give greater attention to the commercialization of Pateh while preserving its authenticity and quality. Market-oriented designs, professional packaging, standardization, marketing and the creation of sales networks could increase the added value of Pateh products and strengthen the livelihoods of artisans, he said.
Professional and targeted branding is also essential for promoting Pateh nationally and internationally, Farrokhi added. A distinctive visual identity, a clear narrative, a credible trademark and an appropriate marketing strategy could make the craft more recognizable in target markets and facilitate its entry into international markets.
“Pateh of Kerman has the potential to become a well-known brand,” he said, adding that artisans, economic operators, private-sector companies and specialized handicraft organizations could work together to establish an integrated production, distribution and export chain.
Farrokhi cautioned that expanding the Pateh market should not come at the expense of its authenticity. The goal, he said, should be to balance preservation of the craft’s identity with contemporary market demands by maintaining traditional characteristics while developing diverse and functional products suited to modern consumers.
He also called for greater promotion of Pateh through specialized exhibitions, cultural events and target markets, as well as through e-commerce and new media. Stronger links between artisans and commercial operators, along with improved packaging and presentation, could further raise awareness of the craft in domestic and international markets.
Pateh Douzi, in which colorful stitches cover much of the surface of a textile, is among Kerman’s best-known traditional handicrafts and souvenirs.
Because textiles are vulnerable to deterioration, few ancient Pateh pieces have survived. However, accounts and travel documents dating to the Safavid era indicate that the craft was practiced in Kerman at the time. One of the oldest surviving examples, dating to around 1869, was used as a cover for the tombstone of Shah Nematollah Vali, the Persian Sufi master and poet who lived in the 14th and 15th centuries.
Farrokhi described Pateh as a “living heritage” that connects Kerman’s past with the present and future, saying intelligent branding and commercialization could help transform the traditional art from a valued local craft into a product with a recognizable international identity.

====================================================================================================

Study finds AI chatbots can conduct basic therapy sessions but struggle to adapt to individual needs
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
15:00:39 UTC
00:00:39 KST

A recent study published in the journal Computers in Human Behavior: Artificial Humans suggests that artificial intelligence chatbots can conduct basic therapeutic conversations, though they struggle to consistently apply specific treatment techniques. The research indicates that a language model performed similarly to human practitioners in high-quality research settings, but its ability to adapt specialized psychological methods to individual needs remains inconsistent.
The global shortage of accessible mental health care has led experts to consider technology as a possible bridge. According to global health estimates cited by the study authors, hundreds of millions of individuals live with mental health conditions, yet structural barriers like cost and geographic distance leave the vast majority without professional care.
For example, the authors of a 2024 framework published in npj Mental Health Research argued that large language models hold tremendous potential to expand access to personalized treatments. A large language model (also known as an LLM) is a type of artificial intelligence trained on vast amounts of text, allowing it to generate human-like responses to prompts.
The authors of the 2024 paper proposed a roadmap for integrating these systems into clinical care, noting that psychotherapy is a high-stakes environment requiring nuanced expertise and responsible development. Despite this optimism, the actual clinical skills of these language models have remained largely untested in real-world scenarios. Previous research mostly relied on giving chatbots brief, fictional scenarios and asking human judges to rate the isolated responses for empathy or helpfulness.
These brief tests provide little insight into whether a computer program can manage a full, goal-directed therapy session that requires a coherent strategy. In a real session, a practitioner must dynamically adjust to the user’s changing emotional state and guide the conversation toward a beneficial outcome. This gap in knowledge motivated the authors of the new study to evaluate how well a language model actually performs when conducting a live, uninterrupted therapy session with a human user.
“Previous research had mostly examined LLM-chatbots’ skill at doing therapy by having them generate brief therapeutic responses or short excerpts of sessions, often in reply to a narrow set of client problems,” said study author Arthur Bran Herbener, a researcher at the Department of Psychology and Behavioral Sciences at Aarhus University. “We needed research that examines how skillfully LLM-chatbots can carry out full cognitive behavioral therapy sessions and adapt treatment to different individuals, using well-established, standardized metrics of therapeutic competence.”
To explore this question, the researchers designed an observational study involving 65 university students. These participants were experiencing mild to moderate psychological distress, such as presentation anxiety, occasional worrying, or perfectionism. Students with severe distress or diagnosed mental health disorders were excluded to ensure participant safety, as language models can sometimes generate unpredictable or inappropriate responses. Each participant completed a single 30-minute in-person session with a locally hosted artificial intelligence chatbot, exchanging an average of 49 messages.
The chatbot was programmed to deliver Cognitive Behavioral Therapy, a widely used, problem-oriented treatment that helps individuals identify and change unhelpful thoughts and behaviors. The researchers configured a specific language model to progress through the typical phases of a session. Rather than giving the chatbot a static set of rules, the researchers used a secondary background model to monitor the ongoing conversation. This secondary model evaluated the elapsed time and the current context, guiding the primary chatbot to shift from establishing a connection to conceptualizing the problem, applying an intervention, and finally wrapping up the conversation politely.
After the sessions concluded, trained graduate students read the conversation transcripts and rated the chatbot’s performance using the Cognitive Therapy Scale. This standardized assessment tool measures two main areas of clinical proficiency. The first area covers general therapeutic skills, such as expressing warmth and understanding. The second area covers specific skills, such as applying targeted techniques and guiding the user toward new insights.
To provide a benchmark for the chatbot’s scores, the researchers also conducted a meta-analysis of 18 prior studies that used the exact same scale to evaluate human practitioners. A meta-analysis is a statistical technique that combines the data from multiple independent studies to find an overall average or trend. This technique allowed the researchers to compare the chatbot’s performance to an established baseline of human competence.
The researchers found that the chatbot’s overall competence score fell slightly below the generally accepted threshold for adequate clinical performance. The standard rating scale defines an acceptable level of competence as a score of 40 out of a possible 66 points. The chatbot achieved this minimum threshold in 30 of the 65 sessions, showing a high degree of variability from one conversation to the next.
“We were surprised by how much variation the LLM-chatbot showed in its skillfulness across CBT sessions,” Herbener told PsyPost. “This is an important observation, as it suggests that we need research to ensure consistently competent care across individuals, and to understand when and why performance dips.”
When compared to the broad pool of human practitioners from the meta-analysis, the chatbot scored somewhat lower overall. The human professionals scored an average of 40.3 points on the rating scale, compared to the chatbot’s adjusted score of 38.1 points. However, the performance gap disappeared when the researchers looked only at the most rigorously conducted human studies. Compared to the six human studies judged to be of high methodological quality, the chatbot’s scores exhibited no statistically significant difference.
A closer look at the types of skills displayed by the chatbot highlighted a distinct pattern in its capabilities. The artificial intelligence performed better than human practitioners in general therapeutic skills, such as communicating empathy, validating the user’s feelings, and fostering a collaborative environment. In contrast, the chatbot struggled with the specific, technical skills required for this highly structured type of therapy. It had difficulty reliably identifying key beliefs, guiding the user through self-discovery, and adapting intervention strategies to fit the unique characteristics of each participant.
These technical struggles highlight the difference between following a predetermined structure and tailoring a method to a specific person. “LLM-chatbots designed to deliver therapy show promise in adhering to the cognitive behavioral treatment protocol, but there may be challenges in adapting to different individuals,” Herbener explained. “It’s also worth noting that good observable skill in delivering therapy is not the same as clinical effectiveness.”
“Effectiveness likely depends on factors beyond observable skill, such as positive expectations and a strong therapeutic relationship,” Herbener continued. “More comprehensive assessments of LLM-chatbots’ therapeutic competence may also require entirely new assessment approaches that account for LLMs’ distinct behavioral tendencies — such as sycophancy, or a tendency to excessively affirm users. Understanding this is crucial, since the ability to challenge clients’ beliefs is often considered important for fostering clinical change.”
These findings do not indicate that artificial intelligence is ready to replace human practitioners. The study evaluated the chatbot based on a single session with young adults experiencing only mild distress. Clinical populations often present more complex challenges, including severe hopelessness or safety risks. These complex cases place much higher demands on a practitioner’s ability to adapt and respond skillfully to unpredictability.
A single session also cannot capture the long-term planning, homework review, and relationship-building required in a complete, multi-week treatment program. In addition, the study faced challenges in consistently rating the chatbot’s text-based transcripts. The standard rating scale was originally designed for video or audio recordings, where raters can hear tone of voice and observe body language. Applying this tool to text may have made certain interpersonal skills harder to judge accurately.
Raters also knew they were evaluating an artificial intelligence, which may have influenced their scoring. In real-world applications, this lack of blinding reflects how users will actually interact with known computer systems, but it complicates direct comparisons to human professionals.
The researchers also caution against drawing overly broad conclusions from the matched scores between the chatbot and high-quality human studies. “Showing similar competence levels in CBT between human therapists and an LLM-chatbot does not mean they are equally effective ‘therapists’, nor that they are equally competent in an absolute sense,” Herbener said.
Because the human data came from past studies rather than a side-by-side test, the comparison remains indirect. “We did not directly compare the chatbot and human therapists in an experimental setting, so several factors beyond competence could bias our measurements,” Herbener added. “For example, the severity and type of psychological problems presented, or the norms for inferring behavioral observations clinicians and researchers apply when using the competence scale we relied on.”
To advance the field, future research will need to examine how chatbots perform across multiple sessions with clinical populations and investigate exactly why they sometimes fail to apply specific therapeutic techniques. “Research on LLM-chatbots in mental healthcare is still at a very early stage,” Herbener said. “Alongside design efforts aimed at ensuring consistently competent care, I’m also working to better understand the therapeutic relationship in LLM-based mental healthcare.”
Even if a chatbot can say all the right things, a user’s awareness that they are talking to a machine might alter the impact of those words. “We not only need to know how well these systems mimic human therapists’ language. We also need to understand what meaning clients attribute to that language once they know it comes from a machine,” Herbener explained. “Does the positive regard expressed by a chatbot serve the same clinical function as when it comes from a human? That’s one of the big open questions in the field.”
The study, “Exploring the therapeutic competencies of large language models: Observational study and comparison with meta-analytical estimates for human therapists,” was authored by Arthur Bran Herbener, Robert Zachariae, Michal Klincewicz, Mikkel Berg Thøgersen, Marie Rosenkrantz Hermann, and Malene Flensborg Damholdt.

Mainland China’s ship activity near Taiwan hits record for third month in a row
Beijing has ramped up its law-enforcement and survey operations around island since Tokyo and Manila announced boundary talks
Taiwan’s defence ministry said that in the first 24 days of August it detected mainland coastguard, survey and other official vessels a total of 118 times, surpassing the 117 reported in July and 111 in June.
Those numbers were far higher than in any previous month on record since Taiwan began releasing detection data in July 2024. Before June, the highest count of mainland government ships in a single month was 50 in April 2025, according to a tally by the SCMP.
That record-breaking intensity of activity aligns with findings from an August 19 report by the Asia Maritime Transparency Initiative (AMTI), which noted a “more sustained and assertive presence” by mainland Chinese law-enforcement and survey vessels in waters east of Taiwan.
Using commercial automatic identification system data, AMTI – a programme of the Washington-based Centre for Strategic and International Studies – showed that the ships’ activity around Taiwan between June and August was concentrated in waters where Chinese, Japanese and Philippine maritime claims overlap. Almost no such activity had been observed in that area between January and late May.
Two China Coast Guard vessels accounted for much of the observable activity during that period, while a third had been patrolling the area since August 4, according to AMTI. Within days of the Japan-Philippines announcement, mainland Chinese law-enforcement vessels hailed 198 commercial ships in the area, demanding information about their ports of call and crews, the report noted.

====================================================================================================

Heat therapy for sick frogs lowers sperm levels
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
15:00:00 UTC
00:00:00 KST

Bad news for frogs getting sauna therapy: The heat treatment for their fatal fungal infection may come at a cost.
Exposing infected green and golden bell frogs (Ranoidea aurea) to thermal therapy — a commonly used treatment technique — can compromise their sperm levels. While the frogs became disease free, their sperm concentrations substantially dropped and remained that way months later, researchers report August 4 in the Journal of Experimental Biology.
“We’ve looked at the survival of frogs after these treatments… but it’s less common that we’ve taken into account effects on reproduction,” says Cori Richards-Zawacki, an amphibian biologist at the University of Pittsburg who wasn’t involved in the work. “That seems important,” she says, if the idea is to revive populations “by treating frogs and then putting them back into the wild.”
Over the past half century or so, at least 500 amphibian species — including R. aurea — have suffered population declines driven in part by a chytrid fungus called Bd (Batrachochytrium dendrobatidis). Chytrid colonizes the animals’ skin and disrupts the uptake of electrolytes, which on depletion can ultimately cause cardiac arrest.
Reproductive and conservation biologist Rose Upton witnessed a chytrid outbreak firsthand in July 2024 at a captive breeding facility housing bell frogs in Australia’s University of Newcastle, where she works.
To treat infected frogs, scientists often opt for strategies including antifungal baths and heat therapy. Temperatures above 29° Celsius (84° Fahrenheit) can kill the fungus in days, or even within a few hours at 37° C (99° F). Green and golden bell frogs naturally prefer temperatures around 29° C.
At the Newcastle facility, Upton and colleagues raised the temperature of the cabinets that housed frogs by 2° C every day from 25° C to 37° C, before holding the temperature for six hours and ramping it down in similar increments. While treating the frogs, Upton’s team wanted to understand whether the therapy posed a reproductive cost for bell frogs, given heat stress can impair fertility in many animals, including humans.
The researchers collected sperm from 13 mature male frogs — four Bd negative and nine Bd positive — before and five weeks after the heat treatment. They found that irrespective of the frogs’ infection status, their sperm concentrations nearly halved after the thermal therapy. Six months later, these levels remained substantially reduced in four infected individuals that were tested again, which came as the “biggest surprise,” Upton says. The team also found that, post-treatment, frogs had a higher proportion of motile sperm, though these levels declined slightly months later.
“I would be a little bit cautious in interpreting these results,” Richards-Zawacki says, given that “we don’t have a really good handle on what causes fluctuations in sperm production in amphibians.” It’s especially hard to say whether the longer-term findings can be attributed to the heat treatment, the lingering effects of the infection, or something else, she adds.
Upton acknowledges that the study is preliminary. It lacks a comparative frog population that didn’t undergo heat treatment; it didn’t include female frogs; and it didn’t track whether heat-treated males produce fewer offspring. It’s also unclear how sperm concentrations and motility may change if infected frogs were exposed to lower temperatures that can also kill the fungus.
“I’m certainly not suggesting that we should stop heat treatments or other mitigative actions,” Upton says. “I think it’s just good to be monitoring reproduction so that we know ahead of time if there is a dip in that fertility.”

US Apache helicopters involved in five major crashes this year — TV
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
14:58:26 UTC
23:58:26 KST

WASHINGTON, August 24. /TASS/. The US Army has seen a significant number of Apache helicopter accidents since last October, which marks the start of fiscal year 2026, CNN reported.
The channel said that five major accidents occurred during the reported period, including a fatal crash that claimed the lives of two people on board and sparked a wildfire. A "major accident" is classified as a mishap that results in either: human casualties, permanent disabilities, total destruction of an aircraft, or losses exceeding $2.5 million.
The AH-64 Apache model was rolled out in 2011. In fiscal year 2024, it saw the most accidents - nine.
On August 14, the US Ground Forces stopped training flights of Apache helicopters, but resumed them on August 20. According to CNN, serious transmission issues were identified in 2024, which in some cases could knock out the aircraft’s tail rotor.

Ukraine has issued a unique commemorative silver coin marking the 35th anniversary of Independence
Ukraine
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
17:58:22 EEST
23:58:22 KST

Ukraine has issued a unique commemorative silver coin marking the 35th anniversary of Independence
Kyiv • UNN
The 20-hryvnia coin is made of .925 sterling silver, with a mintage of up to 5,000 pieces. Its design symbolizes the protection of Ukraine’s freedom.
On Ukraine’s Independence Day, August 24, 2026, the National Bank of Ukraine put into circulation a commemorative coin “To the 35th Anniversary of Ukraine’s Independence,” UNN reports, citing the NBU.
Details
The coin continues the “Ukrainian State” series and is dedicated to the 35th anniversary of the proclamation of Ukraine’s independence—one of the most important events in the modern history of Ukrainian statehood, which embodied the Ukrainian people’s thousand-year aspiration for freedom.
The presentation of this unique silver commemorative coin took place in the Verkhovna Rada of Ukraine. It was here that, 35 years ago, on August 24, 1991, the Act of Proclamation of Ukraine’s Independence was adopted.
“For the National Bank, every commemorative coin is an opportunity to speak through the language of symbols, remind people of what is important, and preserve memory. When we were looking for the image that most accurately embodies 35 years of Ukraine’s Independence, we chose a shield. It has been struck, but it has not broken; it has endured. This is a metaphor for Ukraine. And the hand of a Ukrainian warrior firmly holding this shield reminds us that independence is protected by people, by Ukrainian soldiers. They defend it for the sake of a future symbolized by a child’s drawing—simple, like all true values: a child, a heart, the sun, a home. We bow our heads before those who gave their lives for these values. We thank those who are defending them now,” emphasized Andriy Pyshnyy, Governor of the National Bank of Ukraine.
“Today demonstrated a very important component of our unity. Different parliamentarians, different people, different eras, but everyone has the same goal—to do everything so that Ukraine endures and Ukraine wins. This is the leitmotif for each of us. Great goals lie ahead of us, and we will certainly achieve them. And the grandeur of this moment must be preserved in symbols that will carry this information to future generations, including in Ukrainian currency,” noted Ruslan Stefanchuk, Chairman of the Verkhovna Rada of Ukraine.
About the coin’s design
The commemorative coin “To the 35th Anniversary of Ukraine’s Independence” has a face value of 20 hryvnias and is made of .925 sterling silver; its minting quality category is “special uncirculated,” and the weight of the precious metal in pure form is 62.2 g. The coin’s edge is smooth with recessed inscriptions. The coin is decorated with patination and selective gilding. Its mintage is up to 5 000 pieces.
The obverse of the coin depicts the hand of a serviceman holding a shield. At the top is an image of a child’s drawing that serves as a talisman for the defender. In the upper-left section is the inscription УКРАЇНА, while on the right are the small State Emblem of Ukraine, the year of minting—2026, the denomination 20, and the graphic hryvnia symbol ₴. The circular inscription reads: 35 РОКІВ БОРОТЬБИ ЗА МАЙБУТНЄ.
The reverse depicts the outer side of a shield with damage, symbolizing the struggle for the freedom and independence of the Ukrainian people.
Artist—Andriy Sahach.

Iran to put 199 agro-processing projects into operation during Government Week
TEHRAN- The Head of the Center for Development of Mechanization and Agricultural Industries has announced the inauguration of 199 agro-processing and food industry projects during Government Week.
According to IRNA from the Ministry of Agriculture, Karim Zolfaghari added: "These projects have the capacity to absorb and process approximately 1.82 million tons of agricultural raw materials and will create employment for 12,427 people, marking an important step in increasing the productivity of agricultural products."
The Head of the Center for Development of Mechanization and Agricultural Industries stated: "In addition to the projects set to be inaugurated during Government Week, 761 semi-finished agricultural industry projects with physical progress exceeding 50% are currently underway across the country. If financial resources are secured and these projects are completed and operationalized, the capacity to absorb and process about 10 million tons of agricultural raw materials will be added to the country's industrial capacity."
He added that in line with developing the capacity of agricultural industries and completing the production process, over 16,700 operating licenses have been issued so far. "These units collectively have the capacity to absorb more than 114 million tons of agricultural raw materials and create employment for approximately 270,000 people," he said.
Regarding the ranking of provinces in terms of projects to be inaugurated during Government Week, Zolfaghari specified: "In terms of investment value, the provinces of West Azarbaijan, Mazandaran, and Markazi rank first to third, respectively; and in terms of the number of projects, Khuzestan, West Azarbaijan, and Kerman rank first to third, respectively."
He added: "In terms of job creation, West Azarbaijan, Khuzestan, and Mazandaran have secured the top ranks in the country."
The Head of the Center for Development of Mechanization and Agricultural Industries described the development of agro-processing and food industries as one of the most important tools for preventing raw material sales, reducing waste, and increasing the added value of agricultural products, stating: "We must act in such a way that a greater share of the country's production, after processing and packaging, is offered to domestic and export markets with higher added value."
He said: "Processing industries can transform the country's agricultural production advantage into an export advantage, especially since global markets are moving toward high-quality, standardized products with proper packaging and traceability."
Zolfaghari added: "Upgrading technology, smartening production and processing processes, improving packaging, increasing productivity, and complying with export standards are requirements for the development of processing industries, and investment in this sector must receive greater attention than in the past."

China delays ambitious Chang’e 7 moon mission until 2027
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
14:56:38 UTC
23:56:38 KST

China’s lunar ambitions took a hit after the country called off the launch of a spacecraft that it has aimed to make the first to ever land on the moon’s south pole.
Chang’e 7 was supposed to launch onboard a Long March 5 rocket on August 24, but the launch was scrubbed on Sunday evening, China’s Manned Space Agency (CMSA) said in a statement. Poor weather conditions linked to Typhoon Narra were likely a factor. As a result, the mission will be put on ice until at least 2027.
The decision to delay the mission was reached after extensive review, the CMSA said, and reflects the need for “absolute safety.”
On supporting science journalism
If you're enjoying this article, consider supporting our award-winning journalism by subscribing. By purchasing a subscription you are helping to ensure the future of impactful stories about the discoveries and ideas shaping our world today.
China and the U.S. are in a race to send humans back to the moon, a feat that that U.S. accomplished in the 1960s and that has never been achieved by any other country. Chang’e 7 is to play to a crucial part in China’s ambitions: the probe is designed to search for frozen water in the Shackleton Crater, a pit that spans some 21 kilometers (13 miles) and sits beside the lunar south pole.
No spacecraft has ever landed in that region, which scientists consider to be among the likeliest places to find water on the moon. Locating water that can be used by humans is vital to establishing any long-term lunar presence. NASA and China are eyeing the region to establish a crewed base—and because this real estate is so precious, it matters who gets their first.
NASA plans to establish a lunar colony by 2036, while China hopes to begin construction of the International Lunar Research Station, a moon base built in collaboration with Russia, in 2031. The CMSA has stated it aims to put humans on the lunar surface no later than 2030, while NASA’s Artemis IV mission to land humans on the moon for the first time in more than half a century is tentatively scheduled for early 2028.
