Surprise protests, GenZ outreach: Rahul Gandhi's new playbook after CJP's success
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
14:55:23 UTC
23:55:23 KST

Surprise protests, GenZ outreach: Rahul Gandhi's new playbook after CJP's success
Priyanka Mukherjee / TIMESOFINDIA.COM / Updated: Aug 24, 2026, 21:30 IST
Comments
Share
AA
Text Size
Small
Medium
Large
Rahul Gandhi's new playbook shows a more persistent style, combining youth outreach, and surprise street protests.
NEW DELHI: "I will not leave till you lodge an FIR. He has pellet injuries on his body, has lost his eye, and we are demanding an FIR for this… I will go outside and sit there until you lodge an FIR. I will sit for 10 days, 20 days, 30 days, 50 days, six months. Your government will have a problem."There is a visible change in how Rahul Gandhi pushes through issues now. The leader of the opposition in Lok Sabha has a new weapon in his political arsenal: impromptu protests.For the second time in a month, the Congress leader caught the authorities and, to some extent, even his own party, off guard by taking to the streets of the national capital without prior warning.When Rahul reached the DCP office in Delhi on August 21 demanding an FIR for Sahil Lohchab, who sustained pellet injuries during the July 20 student protest, it was Act 2 of his new playbook of street protest politics.When the cops dilly-dallied, Rahul refused to budge and staged a sit-in protest outside the police station. Priyanka Gandhi Vadra joined him. Congress leaders began arriving. Rapid Action Force personnel were deployed outside.
A visit to a police office turned into a high-voltage political stand-off and, after six hours of dharna, Delhi Police registered an FIR over the pellet firing.
'Politics of anarchy'
The BJP reacted sharply, calling Rahul's sit-in the 'politics of anarchy'. It argued that an FIR relating to the wider July 20 incident already existed, an investigation was under way and the Congress leader was turning police procedure into a political spectacle.For Lohchab, however, there was a sense of relief. His family had been trying to get his complaint registered since August 14, shuttling between police stations. The 19-year-old, who underwent surgery after pellets struck his right eye, finally had an FIR registered after Rahul’s intervention.
According to Lochav's lawyer, the police returned the complaint after reading it and made them wait for a long time.
For years, opponents have labelled Rahul Gandhi a 'part-time politician' who would raise an issue and then disappear. But this incident reinforced something that has changed about Rahul’s tactics over the past year. He is no longer content merely to raise an issue. He increasingly wants to enter it, stay with it and, if necessary, physically occupy the space.
Rahul's new playbook
The first display of this surprise street-politics instinct had come a month earlier during the NEET student protests.On July 21, a day after police used force to stop thousands of students attempting a 'Sansad Chalo' march over exam irregularities, Rahul suddenly headed towards Prime Minister Narendra Modi’s residence and staged a sit-in.Police had reportedly made arrangements anticipating a Congress protest at Vijay Chowk. Rahul went to Lok Kalyan Marg instead. The move caught security officials by surprise. Rahul, Priyanka and several other opposition leaders were later detained. The visual that immediately took over the news was hard to ignore: the leader of opposition physically struggling with police officers, being dragged away and getting a nosebleed in the chaos.
Police personnel detained Rahul Gandhi during a sit-in protest near the Prime Minister's residence.
That was Act 1.It came at a time when the Congress was desperately trying to make its presence felt, with Abhijeet Dipke’s Cockroach Janata Party and climate activist Sonam Wangchuk already leading a massive student protest against the NEET paper leak.Congress had initially kept its distance, preferring to run its own youth outreach campaign, 'Chhatron Ki Goonj'. But people asked a simple question: If Congress claimed to support student issues, why was it missing from the biggest student movement in Delhi?When the July 20 Jantar Mantar protest took place and massive outrage erupted over police action against the student protesters, Rahul's approach changed.The very next day, he moved to the front lines. He stood outside the Prime Minister's house, Congress leaders visited injured students, and police action became a major national debate. Rahul took the issue to Parliament and continued raising the pitch over injuries suffered by students.And when the larger protest began to recede from the headlines, Rahul stayed with one part of it: the use of pellet guns. He has affirmed that his party will keep raising the issue and demand accountability.
Rahul's changing style
The first major image makeover was when Rahul Gandhi embarked on the 'Bharat Jodo Yatra' from Kanyakumari to Kashmir in 2022. Sceptics and rivals wondered if he would complete the journey. By the time he reached Kashmir months later, after walking thousands of kilometres in his now-signature white T-shirt, one charge had become considerably harder to make: that Rahul was unwilling to put in the physical grind that it takes in politics.The yatra, on its own, did not help the Congress much. What it did was change the way Rahul himself was seen as a politician.For months, people saw him walking with the common public rather than delivering a scripted speech from a podium. He met farmers, labourers and students, stopped for conversations, entered homes and sat and ate with people.The yatra experience also laid the foundation for his growing social media popularity. His online presence today increasingly relies on short, conversational exchanges that can easily travel as reels and clips.The second shift came in 2024, when he became the leader of the opposition.Since then, we have seen a more combative Rahul Gandhi. Whether one agrees with his politics or the issues he raises, he appears considerably more comfortable with confrontation than he once did.Congress's protests in the Parliament complex have also become more visual and experimental under Rahul.He repeatedly joined opposition MPs at Makar Dwar over the Election Commission’s SIR exercise in Bihar, with MPs carrying 'vote chori' placards and demanding its rollback. The protest later moved beyond the Parliament gates, with Rahul and other opposition leaders marching towards the Election Commission before being stopped and detained.Rahul provokes ministers across the aisle and stays for the response. He takes an argument from the street into Parliament and, increasingly, carries it back outside again. This is also a part of his renewed Gen Z outreach.
'Chhatron Ki Goonj'
If Bharat Jodo helped Rahul Gandhi build the image of a politician who was more approachable and willing to listen, 'Chhatron Ki Goonj' took that idea to a more specific audience: Gen Z.The programme began in Kota, where students spoke about paper leaks, recruitment delays, coaching pressure and the uncertainty around exams and jobs. The format was less rally and more conversation, with Rahul asking questions, listening, and allowing individual experiences to shape the message. That made the outreach more relatable.His latest Pune edition further widened that outreach by putting young women at the centre of the conversation.
Rahul's call to 'smash the patriarchy' in a stadium full of women was a strong message that came with its own set of criticism. He spoke about different social expectations for boys and girls, cited the 'Manusmriti' while attacking the BJP-RSS worldview, and told women that they 'belong' to themselves rather than to fathers, husbands or sons, triggering huge applause from the crowd.While a large section of people lauded the focus on women, the ruling BJP immediately trained its guns on him and asked him to induct women ministers into the Karnataka cabinet, where Congress is in power.
The Jharkhand dilemma
Even as Rahul attacked the BJP over issues such as NEET and paper leaks, major student protests broke out in Jharkhand, where the Congress shares power in the Hemant Soren government. Soon after the Jantar Mantar agitation ended, aspirants took to the streets in Ranchi over alleged irregularities and paper leaks in JPSC and JSSC examinations.
The BJP MP dared Rahul Gandhi to take a decisive political stand instead of limiting his activism to social media platforms.
This time, the criticism was closer to home. Protesters targeted not just chief minister Hemant Soren but Rahul as well, pointing to the irony of him speaking for students nationally while Congress was part of the ruling alliance in Jharkhand. Effigies of both leaders were burnt, and Rahul was accused of not speaking about Jharkhand issues.Eventually, Rahul was forced to react and intervene. He asserted that Congress supported students protesting peacefully and said that dialogue was the only way forward.
Rahul Gandhi urged Jharkhand CM Hemant Soren to personally meet representatives of students protesting irregularities in state recruitment examinations and address their remaining concerns.
Rahul wrote an open letter to Soren, requesting the CM to personally meet the students and hear out their grievances. The Jharkhand government later accepted the students' demands.The episode helped Rahul avoid the charge of being selective on student issues, but it also showed the challenge of claiming an issue as your own: once you do that, you can be questioned even when the government under fire is run by an ally.
Has Rahul evolved as a leader?
For years, Rahul Gandhi was accused of being an inconsistent politician who would raise important issues and then disappear from the conversation. Rivals branded him as an “entitled leader” and took “Pappu” digs at him.
But the old image of Rahul as a reluctant politician is certainly harder to sustain today. From Bharat Jodo to 'Chhatron Ki Goonj', fiery parliamentary confrontations and surprise dharnas, he appears more comfortable playing on the front foot.However, his latest round of street protests seems to stem from the crisis that the opposition in general and Congress in particular faced as Abhijeet Dipke-led CJP mobilised youth in large numbers and forced the government to make Dharmendra Pradhan resign as Union education minister.Rahul has made his presence felt, but we will have to wait and see if his new politics translates into electoral success for the Congress.
Priyanka Mukherjee is an Assistant News Editor at The Times of India, where deadlines rarely slow down. Often juggling breaking news, long-form explainers and the everyday chaos of the newsroom, she is usually managing stress, chasing clarity and trying to log off on time (rarely successful). She also cares deeply about stories around women’s rights and social change, while two very opinionated dogs make sure she never truly switches off.

‘We don’t need Canada’: Trump threatens to double auto tariffs after trade talks collapse
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
14:55:13 UTC
23:55:13 KST

‘We don’t need Canada’: Trump threatens to double auto tariffs after trade talks collapse
US tariffs on all Canadian cars and trucks, automotive parts and steel will be increased to 50% starting January 1, 2027, President Donald Trump said in a social post on Monday. Doubling down on his threats, Trump said the US does not “need” Canada after trade talks between the two countries collapsed over the weekend.
To display this content from YouTube, you must enable advertisement tracking and audience measurement.
One of your browser extensions seems to be blocking the video player from loading. To watch this content, you may need to disable it on this site.
US President Donald Trump said Monday that he would double tariffs on Canadian vehicles beginning in 2027, as the neighbouring countries spiral towards an escalating trade war.
"On January First, 2027, Tariffs on all Cars, Trucks, both large and small, Automotive Parts, and Steel, will be increased to 50%," he wrote on his Truth Social platform.
The current US tariff on autos stands at 25% for non-US content, while imported steel to the United States generally faces a 50% duty.
Trump's latest threat comes after the North American neighbours failed Friday to reach a deal to avert new 50% tariffs imposed by the Trump administration on select Canadian goods.
Read moreTrump says Canada wants 'benefits of being a State, without being one' as trade war deepens
The White House had alleged "discriminatory treatment" by Canada against US alcohol, automobile and dairy products in rolling out the duties.
Trump delayed their implementation by three days last week as Washington and Ottawa stepped up negotiations towards a trade pact.
However, the steep duties took effect Saturday as both sides failed to reach an agreement, and it hits about 5.5% of Canadian goods entering the United States, or some $20 billion worth of products ranging from hockey sticks to cement.
Carney slams 'economic integration as a weapon'
Canadian Prime Minister Mark Carney has since announced retaliatory tariffs on the United States after walking away from what he called a "bad deal."
Read moreCanada vows to match US tariffs 'dollar for dollar' after failed trade negotiations
Carney accused Washington of using “economic integration as a weapon” and said “its signature was written in pencil”. Resorting to the language of battle, he said his country had been “attacked” by the new American tariffs. “You’re at war when you get attacked,” he said, adding that Canada had the reserves, resilience and plan to respond.
But to Trump’s chief trade negotiator, Jamieson Greer, the US was compelled to act after a year of retaliation by its longtime partner.
“We’ve said enough, and so we’ve taken countermeasures. Our interest is in protecting American workers and protecting American supply chains,” the US trade representative told “Fox & Friends Weekend”.
Carney said Canada had been willing to drop remaining retaliatory tariffs on steel, aluminum and autos if the US substantially lowered its own, and to encourage provinces to restore US alcohol sales. But he said Washington’s final demands went too far. “They asked too much and offered too little,” Carney said.
Greer said the Republican administration was offering to cut tariffs on steel, autos and lumber, “things that are sensitive for them. And they’ve always had the best deal, and they still would have an even better deal, but they didn’t want that.”
As a result, he said, “We’re moving forward with measures that respond to Canadian retaliation.”
Carney said the US added last-minute terms that would have reduced tariff relief for Canadian-made vehicles, restricted Canada’s ability to strike trade deals with other countries and weakened protections for language, culture and sovereignty.
He said such demands were “unacceptable”.
The breakdown in negotiations marked a sharp reversal from two days earlier, when officials from the two countries sounded as if they were headed toward a compromise.
Ontario Premier Doug Ford, who leads Canada’s most populous province, praised Carney for rejecting the deal, saying it would have hurt Ontario’s auto, steel and manufacturing sectors. Ford urged Canada to use “every tool in our toolbox” to fight the US tariffs.
The moves also call into question the future of a North American trade agreement covering the United States, Canada and Mexico that is crucial to industry in all three countries.
Carney said the breakdown was “certainly not good news” for the review of that agreement and that the failed negotiations had given Canada “a new perspective” on what Washington wants from the broader economic relationship.
The political impact will likely be even bigger than the economic fallout. The countries sold each other $880 billion worth of goods and services last year.
The US and Canada have wrangled for decades over trade, poking each other over sore spots such as Canadian softwood lumber imports and U.S. access to Canada’s protected dairy market.
Somehow, they still managed to remain friends, allies and trading partners. Canadian soldiers fought alongside Americans in Afghanistan after 9/11. The 5,525-mile US-Canada border is undefended, and nearly 330,000 people and $2 billion worth of goods cross it every day; 800,000 Canadians live in the United States.
Trump’s approach to dealing with Canada marks an extraordinary departure from the traditionally cooperative relationship between the two countries. Trump has imposed tariffs on Canadian goods in a push to bring manufacturing back to the United States and made inflammatory comments about turning Canada into America’s 51st state.
Watch moreTrump says Canadians 'want' to become the 51st US state. Is this even possible?
Carney said Canada had recognised that “America has changed” and that the two countries would “not return to our old relationship”.
(FRANCE 24 with AFP and AP)

Iran discovers major gas field "Takhteh" in Fars province worth $45b
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
14:55:01 UTC
23:55:01 KST

Iran discovers major gas field "Takhteh" in Fars province worth $45b
TEHRAN- The Exploration Director of the National Iranian Oil Company (NIOC) has announced that the newly discovered "Takhteh" gas field in Fars Province has an estimated economic value of approximately $45 billion, describing it as one of the most significant gas discoveries in the country over the past two decades.
In an interview with Shana, Seyyed Mohieddin Jafari detailed the discovery of the Takhteh gas field, stating that it is located 10 kilometers northwest of the city of Khonj and approximately 35 kilometers from the old gas fields of "Shanul" and "Homa."
He explained that the total gas in place at this field amounts to 7,500 billion cubic feet. With a recovery factor of 72%, approximately 5,700 billion cubic feet of this volume is recoverable.
Jafari noted that the gas from the Takhteh field is sweet, emphasizing: "This gas does not require a sweetening process, so it will follow a shorter path in development and processing compared to sour gas fields."
Equivalent to 15 years of production from one phase of South Pars
Jafari stated that the recoverable gas from Takhteh is equivalent to about 15 years of production from a standard phase of South Pars, and added: "This reserve can play a significant role in meeting the country's gas demand."
He announced that the field is about 50 kilometers long and 4 kilometers wide, and described it as "one of the most complex exploration structures discovered in the last 20 years" in terms of geological complexities.
Referring to the field's geological challenges, Jafari explained: "Multiple layer repetitions and faulting in the upstream sections of the reservoir made exploration operations highly difficult. Nevertheless, exploratory drilling continued to a depth of about five kilometers underground, and upon entering the reservoir, a significant volume of gas was discovered in a thickness of approximately 450 meters."
20 million barrels of recoverable condensates
Jafari also reported the presence of 27 million barrels of gas condensates in the Takhteh field, noting that about 20 million barrels of this amount are recoverable. Combined with the gas reserves, this brings the field's economic value to around $40 billion.
Regarding the exploration timeline, he said: "The drilling of the Takhteh exploratory well alone took about 17 months. After completing exploration operations and conducting final tests, the obtained data were carefully analyzed."
The Exploration Director of NIOC further emphasized that the entire exploration process of the Takhteh field was carried out by Iranian personnel, stating: "All exploration stages—from geological studies and geophysical activities to drilling operations—were conducted using the capabilities of Iranian forces."
Jafari added: "The technology, equipment, and tools used in this operation are also Iranian. In technical, equipment, and engineering sectors, there was no need for foreign personnel or companies."
He described the Takhteh gas field as a fully Iranian discovery and stressed: "This achievement demonstrates that the country's technical, engineering, and specialized capacity in oil and gas exploration has reached a level where complex exploration projects can be successfully completed without reliance on foreign parties."
EF/MA

Venice Film Festival adds Gaza doc ‘NAZA’ to competition lineup
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
14:53:01 UTC
23:53:01 KST

Venice Film Festival adds Gaza doc ‘NAZA’ to competition lineup
The Venice Film Festival has added a new documentary about the civilian toll of the Israel-Hamas war to its lineup. “NAZA,” from two of the filmmakers behind the Oscar-winning documentary “No Other Land,” will screen in the main competition, festival organizers said Monday.
According to the synopsis, the film was shot at night on Tel Aviv rooftops and focuses on the killings of Palestinian civilians. Israeli filmmakers Yuval Abraham and Rachel Szor co-directed “Naza,” which was produced by The Guardian and filmmakers behind “The Zone of Interest.” It’s the only documentary competing for the Golden Lion award.
Abraham and Szor were two of the four directors of the 2025 best documentary winner “No Other Land,” the story of Palestinian activists fighting to protect their communities from demolition by the Israeli military. A collaboration between Israeli and Palestinian filmmakers, it was made over four years between 2019 and 2023, wrapping production days before Hamas launched its deadly Oct. 7, 2023 attack on Israel that started the war in Gaza.
“Naza” builds on reporting between 2023 and 2025 published by the Israeli-Palestinian +972 Magazine, the Hebrew-language outlet Local Call and The Guardian.
The war was top of mind at last year’s festival as well, where thousands gathered for a large pro-Palestinian protest during one of the busiest nights in hopes of redirecting the spotlight from the movie stars to the real world. The subject was also prominent in the program: Kaouther Ben Hania’s “The Voice of Hind Rajab,” about the death of a 6-year-old girl attempting to flee Gaza City with her family in early 2024, won the festival’s Silver Lion award.
Ben Hania is part of the Maggie Gyllenhaal-led jury that will be selecting the award winners at the festival, which begins on Sept. 2. Winners will be announced on Sept. 12.
Earlier this year, the Venice Biennale contemporary art exhibition opened with no Golden Lions after the jury quit in protest of Israel’s and Russia’s participation and loud protests outside their pavilions. In 2024 the artist and curators representing Israel at the Venice Biennale kept the Israeli pavilion exhibit closed, saying they would only open it if there were a ceasefire in Gaza.

Prince Harry to leave board of African Parks conservation nonprofit
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
14:53:01 UTC
23:53:01 KST

Prince Harry to leave board of African Parks conservation nonprofit
LONDON (AP) — Prince Harry is stepping down as a trustee of African Parks, a nonprofit organization that manages protected areas in partnership with authorities in 13 countries from Chad to Zimbabwe.
Harry, who last week announced he and his family were returning to live in the U.K., left the board of trustees after 10 years of service to the organization, a spokesperson for prince confirmed on Monday.
African Parks said in a statement dated Aug. 19 that the prince’s departure was part of a “governance refresh” in which a Kenyan Supreme Court justice and the president of AGRA, which helps small farmers in Africa, will join the board. The changes take effect Sept. 30.
Harry’s departure comes four months after the Indigenous rights group Survival International called on him to resign after it alleged human rights abuses by rangers employed by African Parks. Previously, the prince left Sentebale, the charity he co-founded to fight poverty and AIDS in southern Africa, in March 2025 after relations with the organization’s chair broke down.
“We extend our thanks to all the members of the Board who generously volunteer their time in support of our conservation mission — and particularly to The Duke of Sussex, for a decade of committed service to African Parks,” Vas Narasimhan, chair of the African Parks board, said in a statement.
Harry and his wife, Meghan, stepped aside from royal duties when they moved to the U.S. They will not return to royal duties and are expected to live in a non-royal residence outside London.

====================================================================================================

Ontario’s premier: Canada should be ready to cut electricity, critical minerals as trade woes worsen
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
14:53:01 UTC
23:53:01 KST

Ontario’s premier: Canada should be ready to cut electricity, critical minerals as trade woes worsen
TORONTO (AP) — Ontario Premier Doug Ford said Monday that Ronald Reagan would be “throwing up” over President Donald Trump’s trade policies and threatened to cut off electricity and critical minerals to the United States as Trump escalated the trade fight.
Ford, speaking in an interview with The Associated Press, said Trump has underestimated Canadians’ willingness to endure economic pain rather than give in to U.S. pressure.
“He underestimates Canada. We’re all in,” Ford said. “Up here, we’re at a fever pitch, everyone’s in for an economic war. They know they’re going to have to sacrifice.”
Trump said Monday that the new 50% tariffs on Canadian automobiles, auto parts and steel would begin next year.
“Canada has been ripping off the United States of America for years,” Trump wrote on social media, criticizing what he called Canada’s “ridiculously high tariffs” on American farmers.
In the AP interview, Ford said Trump had “declared war, economic war against his closest friend and ally” before invoking Reagan, whose opposition to tariffs Ford previously featured in a television advertising campaign aimed at Americans.
Ford noted that Trump keeps a portrait of Reagan near his desk.
“He’d be throwing up on him from the picture if he knew what was going on, spinning around in his grave right now,” Ford said. “He should maybe take that picture off the wall and move it somewhere else. Because right now, Ronald Reagan would be disgusted with President Trump.”
Ford’s comments came after Prime Minister Mark Carney walked away from trade negotiations with the Trump administration late Friday, saying Washington demanded too much in exchange for tariff relief. The United States imposed 50% tariffs Saturday on about $20 billion worth of Canadian goods, while Carney announced dollar-for-dollar retaliation beginning Sept. 8.
Ford threatens critical minerals and electricity
Ford said “everything is on the table” if the dispute worsens, including cutting off electricity and critical minerals from Ontario. He also called for Canada to consider using oil and potash as leverage.
“I’ll cut them off,” Ford said of critical minerals. “You won’t get a grain of sand out of Ontario.”
Critical minerals are increasingly important to U.S. national security and manufacturing. The Pentagon has sought more secure supplies of minerals used in military aircraft, missiles, munitions and electronics as Washington tries to reduce reliance on China, which dominates the mining or processing of several strategically important minerals.
Ford specifically cited high-grade nickel shipped to the United States and uranium refined in Ontario.
“What would they do without the high-grade nickel that we ship down to the U.S.?” Ford said.
Ford said Canada should consider increasingly severe retaliation if Trump continues targeting Canadian industries, including oil and potash, while Ontario could raise electricity prices or stop sending power south.
“We power 1.5 million homes and businesses,” Ford said. “Everything’s on the table. I’ll do whatever it takes.”
If Trump continues trying to dismantle Canadian manufacturing, Ford said, “he better have a pack of batteries.”
Ford has used electricity as leverage before. During an earlier phase of the dispute, Ontario imposed a 25% surcharge on electricity exported to Michigan, Minnesota and New York. Trump responded by threatening to double tariffs on Canadian steel and aluminum before both sides backed away.
Auto industry becomes a central battleground
Ford accused Trump of seeking not simply a better trade deal but to hollow out Canadian industries and move production south.
Ford said Trump wants to make Canada a vassal state.
“He wants to bleed out every single sector and bring them down to the U.S.,” Ford said.
The auto sector is especially important to Ontario, the center of Canada’s vehicle manufacturing industry. Plants and suppliers in Ontario are tightly integrated with factories in Michigan and other U.S. states, with parts routinely crossing the border multiple times during production.
Automakers including Ford, General Motors and Stellantis operate major assembly plants in Ontario, and the wider supply chain supports tens of thousands of jobs. Trump’s new threat of a 50% tariff on Canadian vehicles and parts puts that sector directly at the center of the escalating dispute.
U.S. Trade Representative Jamieson Greer said Monday that “the only reason Canada has auto production in the first place” was because of the 1960s Auto Pact, under which Canada used access to its market to encourage vehicle production north of the border.
“If he thinks the Canadian people are going to roll over and just continue buying American-made vehicles, it’s not going to happen,” Ford said.
Ford says he opposed preliminary deal
Ford also disclosed that he had opposed the preliminary agreement Carney was considering before Canada walked away from the negotiations.
“I wasn’t supporting the deal,” Ford said.
Ford said he was unwilling to restore American liquor to Ontario store shelves as part of an agreement and had been prepared to publicly break with the proposed deal.
“I wasn’t going to put the booze back on the shelves,” Ford said. “I was ready to go out there and call a press conference ... and say I’m not buckling over.”
Ford said he understood Washington had sought language late in the negotiations that would have restricted Canada’s ability to negotiate trade agreements with other countries without U.S. approval. Carney has called that demand unacceptable and a question of Canadian sovereignty.
“Who does he think he is?” Ford said of Trump. “You gotta be kidding.”
Reagan fight returns
Ford’s Reagan comments revived an earlier clash with Trump. Last year, Ontario paid for U.S. television advertisements featuring excerpts from a 1987 Reagan address warning that tariffs can provoke retaliation and trade wars.
Trump accused Ontario of misrepresenting Reagan and abruptly ended trade negotiations with Canada. Ford later agreed to pause the campaign so talks could resume.
“He doesn’t respect anyone,” Ford said.
Yet despite the increasingly hostile rhetoric, Ford said Canada should remain willing to negotiate.
“I never believe in walking away from the table,” Ford said. “Continue negotiating and see where we go.”
___
Associated Press writer Seung Min Kim contributed from Washington.

Iran says not to let war end based on "aggressive" parties' conditions
China
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
22:53:00 CST
23:53:00 KST

TEHRAN, Aug. 24 (Xinhua) -- Iran's Foreign Ministry spokesman Esmaeil Baghaei said on Monday that his country will not let the war end based on conditions set by the "aggressive" parties.
"We have definitely not been the party to start the war, but we will not let the war's end be based on the aggressive parties' conditions," Baghaei told a weekly press conference in Tehran.
He said that Iran remained determined to defend its territory and would not "compromise with the aggressive parties" or surrender to their "excessive demands."
Baghaei dismissed U.S. claims that the Strait of Hormuz was open and that millions of barrels of oil were passing through it each day, describing them as part of "psychological and media warfare."
He also slammed Washington's newly announced anti-Iran economic operation, saying Tehran would use all its "multilateral capacities" to counter U.S. sanctions, which he described as a blow to free trade and national sovereignty.
The spokesman also warned that countries assisting U.S. anti-Iran operations could face consequences, and called the U.S. naval blockade an "act of aggression."
Asked about Western media reports that Iran could target U.S. bases in Europe if war resumed, Baghaei said Iran reserved the right to target the origin and source of any "act of aggression" if another country allowed its bases, facilities and equipment to be used for such actions.
Israel and the United States launched joint attacks on Tehran and other Iranian cities on Feb. 28, killing then-Supreme Leader Ali Khamenei, military commanders and civilians. Iran responded with waves of missile and drone attacks targeting Israel and U.S. bases and assets in the region, and tightened its grip on the Strait of Hormuz, barring passage of vessels belonging to or affiliated with Israel and the United States.
A ceasefire was achieved between the warring parties on April 8. On June 18, Iran and the United States signed a memorandum of understanding on ending the war on all fronts, including Lebanon.
U.S. President Donald Trump on Wednesday announced "the most crushing economic operation ever taken" against any country providing a lifeline to Iran, calling the move "an ECONOMIC D-DAY." ■

Muslim-majority Bangladesh voices interest in joining Mecca pact
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
14:53:00 UTC
23:53:00 KST

Bangladesh has expressed interest in joining the Mecca pact, a mutual defense treaty between Saudi Arabia, Türkiye and Pakistan, the country's State Minister for Foreign Affairs Humaiun Kobir said Monday.
Speaking to local media Monday, the state minister said it would not be unusual for Dhaka to join a group of like-minded Islamic countries although no formal proposal has yet to be made.
"We are expressing interest in the Mecca defense pact ... Bangladesh is a country of recognition now and there is an interest," Kobir said, according to leading Bangladeshi daily, Prothom Alo.
“Those who are stakeholders in the Middle East and in the Muslim world – they are inviting Bangladesh’s leadership ... to come and participate," he added.
Humaiun underlined Mecca's importance for Muslim-majority Bangladesh, saying that it was in the hearts of its people.
"If there is a security pact among Islamic nations, it's not unnatural for us to be a part of it. Let's see. We're looking at it positively," he said.
Oil-rich Saudi Arabia, nuclear power Pakistan and Türkiye, which has NATO's second-largest military, signed the Mecca Joint Defense Agreement on Aug. 7.
The mutual defense treaty is aimed at increasing cooperation and deterrence at a time of increased regional uncertainty and threats.
Turkish President Recep Tayyip Erdoğan has already said that the agreement would not be limited to the trio and they would welcome other countries.
"Indeed, our vision is not limited to three countries. We want it to expand further and, if possible, one day bring all countries in the region under this framework," he said in an interview on Aug 12.
"This would be one of the most important steps toward achieving lasting regional stability," said Erdoğan.

Istanbul court announces verdict in opposition mayors’ case

Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
14:52:19 UTC
23:52:19 KST

An Istanbul court on Aug. 24 delivered its verdict in the trial of seven opposition mayors and municipal officials, acquitting businessman Aziz İhsan Aktaş, who had been referred to as the alleged ringleader of a criminal organization, of charges of establishing an organization to manipulate tenders.
The case was brought over allegations that Aktaş bribed mayors and municipal officials to orchestrate public tenders. The court ruled that Aktaş was acquitted of the charges of “establishing a criminal organization.” However, he was sentenced to two years in prison for rigging public tenders and was also banned from participating in public tenders for two years.
The court was issuing separate verdicts for each defendant over their alleged roles in more than 60 acts involving bid-rigging, while the reading of the full verdict was expected to continue until the evening.
Avcılar Mayor Utku Caner Çaykara was acquitted of two charges.
Former Esenyurt Mayor Ahmet Özer was sentenced to eight months and 10 days in prison for “abuse of office through negligence.” Özer was also banned from serving as civil servant.
Beşiktaş Mayor Rıza Akpolat, who was accused of involvement in 52 acts, has so far been acquitted of 18 of them. Akpolat was sentenced to two years and eight months in prison for rigging public tenders and an additional three years for accepting bribes. The reading of the verdicts concerning Akpolat is still ongoing.
The first hearing of the trial was held on Jan. 27, after the indictment was accepted by the court. During the roughly six-month period between the first hearing and July 29, the court held 59 hearings.
The number of defendants in pretrial detention, which stood at 33 when the trial began, has fallen to five following a series of release rulings. A total of 28 defendants, including Adana Mayor Zeydan Karalar,
Adana’s Ceyhan Mayor Kadir Aydar and Istanbul’s Avcılar Mayor Çaykara, Istanbul’s Esenyurt former Mayor Özer, was released pending trial.
However, Istanbul’s Beşiktaş Mayor Akpolat and Adana’s Seyhan Mayor Oya Tekin were jailed pending trial.

BEIJING, Aug. 24 (Xinhua) -- Xi Jinping, general secretary of the Communist Party of China (CPC) Central Committee, has emphasized the importance of consolidating and expanding the progress made in a Party-wide study and education campaign for establishing and practicing a correct understanding of governance performance.
Xi, also Chinese president and chairman of the Central Military Commission, made the remarks in a recent instruction, which was studied at a meeting of the CPC Central Leading Group for Party Building held on Monday.
Xi stressed that establishing and putting into practice a correct view of governance performance is a long-term task that requires sustained efforts.
Since the 18th CPC National Congress, the organization of study and education campaigns across the Party to address major and pressing issues in Party building has become an important institutional arrangement, Xi said, lauding the notable results achieved in the latest campaign.
Xi emphasized the need to regularly conduct education on the correct view of governance performance, guiding Party members and officials to always take responsibility, perform their duties and fulfill their obligations in accordance with such a view.
Efforts should also be made to improve differentiated assessment and evaluation systems, and establish a personnel selection and appointment orientation that values solid work and concrete results, Xi said.
During the meeting, participants thoroughly studied and implemented Xi's instruction, reviewed the campaign, and made arrangements for consolidating and expanding the campaign's outcomes and establishing a long-term mechanism accordingly.
The meeting was chaired by Cai Qi, who is head of the CPC Central Leading Group for Party Building, and was also attended by Li Xi, the group's deputy head. Cai and Li, both of whom are members of the Standing Committee of the Political Bureau of the CPC Central Committee, also delivered speeches.
The meeting noted that the campaign has resolved prominent issues related to deviations in view of governance performance, regulated the official conduct of leading officials at all levels, and given a strong boost to all work aimed at ensuring a good start to the 15th Five-Year Plan period (2026-2030).
The campaign, which started in late February, has basically concluded and aimed to correct misguided views on governance that often breed vanity projects, hidden risks, heavy burdens on local communities, and public discontent. ■

Glimpse of e-commerce giant JD.com's robot data collection center in E China's Jiangsu

A technician collects data for housework scenario at a robot data collection center of Chinese e-commerce giant JD.com in Suqian, east China's Jiangsu Province, Aug. 12, 2026. It upgrades the data collection environment to a real physical scene, transforming the data collection center from a static sample library into a training ground for embodied intelligent robots. (Xinhua/Ji Chunpeng)

====================================================================================================

BeiDou support tops 98 pct of China's H1 phone shipments as commercial adoption broadens
China
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
22:52:08 CST
23:52:08 KST

BEIJING, Aug. 24 (Xinhua) -- More than 98 percent of the 133 million mobile phones shipped in China's domestic market in the first half (H1) of 2026 supported the BeiDou Navigation Satellite System (BDS), as this technology expands across consumer devices, transport and other industries, the Ministry of Industry and Information Technology (MIIT) said on Monday.
Over 14 million shared two-wheelers had adopted BeiDou independent positioning by the end of June, accounting for more than 90 percent of the country's total fleet.
These figures highlight the growing rollout of BeiDou, China's global satellite navigation system, across consumer and industrial applications.
At a media roundtable on large-scale BeiDou applications, Shi Huikang, deputy director of the MIIT's department of electronic information, said China has stepped up pilot programs, product promotion, standards development and ecosystem building in recent years to accelerate adoption.
China's domestic supply base has also continued to expand. By the end of 2025, cumulative shipments of domestically produced BeiDou-compatible chips and modules had approached 2.6 billion units, while the installed base of terminal products with BeiDou functions had exceeded 2.2 billion units, according to the ministry.
Authorities have also supported the development and commercialization of high-precision, interference-resistant and low-power products, helping improve performance and lower costs.
Deployment has also expanded across 39 pilot cities for large-scale BeiDou applications, which had introduced more than 12 million terminals using BeiDou independent positioning by the end of June.
In a number of cities, designated sectors including shared two-wheelers, taxis, government vehicles, agricultural machinery and police equipment have achieved 100-percent BeiDou adoption, the ministry said.
BeiDou's footprint is also expanding across major industries. China now has nearly 40 million BeiDou terminal devices in key sectors, where overall application penetration stands at about 90 percent. More than 160 million wearable devices support BeiDou positioning, while over 100 million passenger vehicles have been equipped with BeiDou-based positioning devices.
Industry data released in May revealed the scale of the broader market. China's satellite navigation industry generated 629 billion yuan (roughly 92.7 billion U.S. dollars) in output in 2025, up 9.24 percent year on year, according to a white paper released by the Global Navigation Satellite System and Location-Based Services Association of China.
The white paper also said BeiDou services and related products have been exported to more than 140 countries and regions worldwide. It said the sector is upgrading toward a broader BeiDou spatiotemporal industry integrating satellite navigation with mobile communications, indoor positioning and artificial intelligence.
During the 15th Five-Year Plan period (2026-2030), the MIIT said it will work with other authorities to support breakthroughs in key technologies and their industrial application, while continuing pilot programs for large-scale BeiDou deployment.
The ministry also plans to foster a broader spatiotemporal information industry by promoting coordinated development across satellite positioning, navigation and timing, remote sensing, geographic information systems, communications and satellite internet. ■

====================================================================================================

Across China: Brazilian programmers infuse passion into Chinese humanoid robots
China
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
22:52:08 CST
23:52:08 KST

BEIJING, Aug. 24 (Xinhua) -- A group of Chinese-made humanoid robots competing for the ball on a soccer field immediately came to a halt when commanded to do so by 21-year-old Brazilian programmer Isabela Moura.
Moments earlier, the robots had been positioning themselves, tracking the ball and kicking autonomously, powered by algorithms developed by young Brazilian programmers and researchers.
For Moura, seeing her work drive Chinese humanoid robots on an international stage is both a validation of her programming skills and a reflection of her passion for robotics.
Moura is a member of Team Brazil, formed this year by young researchers from five Brazilian universities to compete in 5-on-5 humanoid robot soccer at the 2026 World Humanoid Robot Games (WHRG) in Beijing, which brings together 666 teams from 16 countries.
Hailing from Pernambuco in northeastern Brazil, Moura said her passion for humanoid robots was ignited when she joined RoboCIn, a robotics team at the Federal University of Pernambuco, about 18 months ago. In July, she made her first trip abroad to compete at RoboCup in the Republic of Korea before traveling to Beijing.
"It's a really great experience," she said, adding that the robots used by her team are made by Chinese company Booster Robotics, allowing the Brazilian participants to concentrate on software, artificial intelligence and game strategy rather than building a robot from scratch.
"Developing a robot from scratch would require a lot of time devoted to hardware, communications and embedded systems," Moura said. "With a ready-made Chinese platform, we can focus more on what we do best."
This division of work has given the Brazilian team an opportunity to see its algorithms tested on a real-world robotic platform.
"Seeing what we have developed over the past year actually working is extremely rewarding," Moura said.
The effect is also being felt back at the universities in Brazil. When the Chinese robots operate in laboratories, other students often stop to watch, take photos and record videos. Some have become interested in robotics as a result, Moura noted.
For Team Brazil, however, the competition means more than winning games.
"There is competition here, but competition is not the only reason we came," said Joao Aguiar, a member of Team Brazil and a graduate of FEI University Center, who also participated in the competition last year.
The Brazilian team recently played a friendly match against an Italian team. After the game, members of the two teams discussed different approaches to kicking and positioning robots on the field.
"We talk about what each team does differently," Aguiar said. "Through this kind of exchange, all the teams can improve."
With the Chinese teams, the exchange has developed into technical cooperation. A team from Tsinghua University participated in developing part of Team Brazil's code, while China Agricultural University provided support in areas including game strategy.
Cooperation has continued beyond the competition. "We stay in touch, exchange ideas about what we are working on and also discuss maintenance issues," Aguiar said, recalling the relationship he established with Chinese teams during his first visit to Beijing last year.
His experience in China has also deepened his understanding of the country's humanoid robotics industry. During his first trip to China, Aguiar had the opportunity to closely observe humanoid robots developed by Chinese teams.
"The level of development over the past three or four years has been impressive. You can see how robust, fast and intelligent these robots have become in different scenarios, like logistics, industry and pharmacies," he said.
According to Aguiar, Beijing brings together Chinese and international teams in venues where they can develop, train and test robots while exchanging experience and comparing technical solutions. The competition therefore serves not only as a sporting event, but also as a platform for research, development and international exchange.
Beijing, in addition, is strengthening the infrastructure supporting humanoid robotics. The city announced plans to build a training and evaluation base for humanoid robots this year, said Liu Wenqiang, Party secretary of the China Center for Information Industry Development, at a WHRG press conference.
"Beijing has become a hub for humanoid robotics over the past two years," Aguiar said.
For Flavio Tonidandel, coach of Team Brazil and director of Socialdroids Robotics, China's efforts to apply humanoid robots to increasingly complex and practical tasks are particularly noteworthy.
"We need to put robots through increasingly demanding and complex challenges so that they can serve society," he explained.
This approach is reflected in this year's WHRG. In addition to competitive events such as soccer and athletics, the showpiece features 21 scenario-based events covering nine types of real-world needs, namely household and hospitality services, industrial production, emergency response, library management, retail, office services, green-space inspection and vehicle charging.
China is now home to over 140 humanoid robot manufacturers. With an annual shipment volume of 14,400 units, the country now accounts for eight out of every ten humanoid robots produced globally.
Tonidandel believes that clear support policies, competitions such as the WHRG and a steady influx of new talent are important factors behind the rapid development of humanoid robotics in China.
"You see university students, researchers and engineers are all deeply involved in the development of these robots," he said.
As humanoid robots begin to enter real-world applications, Tonidandel also sees greater room for cooperation between Brazil and China.
"Brazilians are faced with some challenges that Chinese may not be, and vice versa. The algorithms we develop in Brazil need to be adapted to local needs in Brazil," he said.
In his view, such cooperation could allow Brazilian researchers to learn from advanced technologies while helping Chinese robots develop solutions that can be applied in more countries.
"This collaboration is very important for both sides. Ultimately, it might facilitate applications of humanoid robots around the world," he said. ■

Putin signs decree on measures to protect critical infrastructure
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
14:51:57 UTC
23:51:57 KST

MOSCOW, August 24. /TASS/. Russian authorities will now have the power to impose temporary management on critical infrastructure facilities, if their owners fail to implement or restore security measures in a timely manner. This provision is established under a new decree signed by President Vladimir Putin.
According to the decree, the Russian government may assume temporary control over critical infrastructure sites, including the fuel and energy sector, industrial enterprises, communication networks, utilities, transport and logistics infrastructure, and energy facilities such as nuclear power plants - when owners neglect security protocols or delay necessary repairs. The measure also applies to life support systems, potentially hazardous facilities, and other sites vital to the nation's security, economic stability, and public well-being.
The decision to implement these measures stems from the increasing security threats faced by Russia's critical infrastructure amid ongoing operations, and aligns with the laws on Martial Law, Defense, and Security. The decree takes effect immediately upon its official publication.

Deregistering a child from their place of residence has become easier — consent from one of the parents can now be obtained through &quot;Diia&quot;
Ukraine
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
17:51:35 EEST
23:51:35 KST

Deregistering a child from their place of residence has become easier — consent from one of the parents can now be obtained through "Diia"
Kyiv • UNN
A child under 14 can be deregistered through "Diia" without the second parent being personally present. A Ukrainian birth certificate, tax identification number, and signature are required.
In Ukraine, deregistering a child from their place of residence has become easier — the consent of one parent can now be obtained online, UNN reports, citing the Diia government services portal.
A child under 14 can now be deregistered from their place of residence online via the Diia portal. One parent can provide consent to deregister the child remotely — their personal presence during the process is no longer required
Parents can use the service if:
✔️the child is under 14; from the age of 14, the application must be submitted independently;
✔️the child has a Ukrainian birth certificate;
✔️both parents are Ukrainian citizens;
✔️they have a tax identification number and Diia.Signature/e-signature.
How does it work?
After logging in to the Diia portal:
1️⃣Go to Services → Change of place of residence → Deregister from the place of residence.
2️⃣Check your details. If necessary, update them in your personal account.
3️⃣Indicate whether you have children and whether you are a person liable for military service.
4️⃣Choose whom you want to deregister: only the child or yourself together with the child.
5️⃣Check the address where you are currently registered.
6️⃣If you are deregistering the child, check their details and provide the details of the other parent.
7️⃣The other parent must provide consent online.
8️⃣Sign the application with a Diia.Signature or electronic signature and pay the administrative fee.
Important for persons liable for military service: you can deregister only the child. The service for simultaneously deregistering yourself and the child is currently unavailable online for persons liable for military service, but it can be obtained at a CNAP

Neighbor accuses Sudan of cross-border airstrikes
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
14:50:47 UTC
23:50:47 KST

Neighbor accuses Sudan of cross-border airstrikes
Chad has accused parties in Sudan’s civil war of carrying out airstrikes deep inside its territory, warning that the conflict risks spilling across the border, the Chadian Armed Forces said on Saturday.
According to the statement, aircraft and drones believed to belong to the Sudanese Armed Forces (SAF) or forces supporting them pursued and bombed a convoy of vehicles on Thursday. The strikes began on Sudanese soil before continuing more than 100 kilometers inside Chad, reaching the Mourdi department in the eastern Ennedi-Est province.
The military said that the extent of the incursion was established following aerial and ground checks conducted on Friday. No Chadian casualties have been reported, but the army condemned what it described as a new violation of the country’s territorial integrity.
Chad warned Sudan’s warring parties against attempting to extend the conflict onto its territory, saying that its defense and security forces remain on the highest level of alert and are prepared to respond to any threat to the country’s security. There has been no response from Sudanese authorities to the Chadian military’s allegations.
Sudan plunged into a civil war three years ago after a struggle for power broke out between the SAF and the paramilitary Rapid Support Forces (RSF).
Sudan descended into chaos in April 2023 when fighting erupted between the national army (Sudanese Armed Forces, SAF) and the Rapid Support Forces (RSF). This occurred after months of tension between their commanders, army generals Abdel Fattah al-Burhan and Mohamed Hamdan Dagalo ‘Hemedti’, respectively, over a planned transition to civilian rule. What began in the capital, Khartoum, as a power struggle has devastated the country, killing tens of thousands and displacing millions.
Regional and international peace efforts, including African Union mediation and Saudi–US talks in Jeddah, have repeatedly stalled. Sudanese officials have named Colombians and Ukrainians among mercenaries backing the RSF against the army. Officials have also accused Ukraine and the United Arab Emirates of involvement and recently claimed the European Union has an “incomplete understanding of the complex situation” in the country.
Khartoum has also accused authorities in neighboring Kenya of backing the RSF and has broken ties with the East African grouping IGAD amid mistrust of regional mediation. In July, TASIS, a political coalition aligned with the paramilitary, announced the formation of a rival government months after its members signed a charter in Nairobi. It named Gen. Dagalo as chairman of a 15-member presidential council, a move rejected by the UN and AU.
The incident follows several previous cases of violence spilling across the Sudan-Chad border. In July, a drone arriving from Sudanese territory struck the eastern Chadian border town of Tine, injuring two children and damaging vehicles and shops, according to local authorities.
In March, a drone strike from Sudan killed at least 17 people in the same town, including mourners attending a funeral. President Mahamat Idriss Deby Itno condemned the attack and ordered the military to retaliate against any future strikes originating from Sudan, whether carried out by the Sudanese army or the RSF.
A month earlier, Chad shut its eastern border with Sudan after five soldiers and three civilians were killed in clashes allegedly involving the SAF and RSF. At least 12 others were wounded in the incident, which also occurred in Tine, officials said.

Two U.N. peacekeepers killed in ambush on patrol in South Sudan
NAIROBI, Aug 24 - Two U.N. peacekeepers were killed in an ambush while on a patrol in South Sudan's Jonglei state on Monday, the U.N. mission said in a statement.
Seven others were injured when unidentified armed men attacked the patrol as it travelled to Pajut, the United Nations Mission in South Sudan said in a statement.
The wounded included three peacekeepers, two South Sudan National Police Service personnel and two civilian staff.
UNMISS said it deployed a Quick Reaction Force to secure the area and support the emergency response.
"Such deadly violence targeting UNMISS personnel who are serving to support peace in South Sudan is unacceptable," Anita Kiki Gbeho, the head of UNMISS, said.
The mission called for a prompt investigation and said attacks on U.N. peacekeepers may constitute war crimes under international law.
Attacks on U.N. personnel are rare but not unprecedented in South Sudan, where UNMISS patrols operate in volatile areas affected by intercommunal fighting, armed youth mobilisations and clashes involving government forces. REUTERS

Estonian shooter wins world clay pigeon gold in sporting first
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
14:47:00 UTC
23:47:00 KST

Estonian shooter wins world clay pigeon gold in sporting first
Shooter Veikko Tihvan has made Estonian sports history by winning the FITASC Compak Sporting World Championships in Rio Salso, Italy.
Tihvan initially shared first place with multiple European and world championship medalist Charles Bardou of France after both hit 197 of 200 targets in Thursday's competition.
A shoot-off beckoned, which Tihvan won after beating the French competitor by one target, making him the first Estonian to win the title.
Clay pigeon shooting is a type of practical hunting shooting which simulates real-world hunting conditions, instead of having flat and static ranges. Not "pigeons" as such, the targets are saucer shaped, made of pulverized limestone and brightly colored.
Compak Sporting is a discipline of clay pigeon shooting governed internationally by FITASC (Fédération Internationale de Tir aux Armes Sportives de Chasse). As its name suggests, it is held on a compact shooting layout designed for use in a smaller area, compared with standard competitive clay pigeon shooting.

Arvind Kejriwal will address a town hall on E20 petrol policy in Goa. Photo credit: ANI
NEW DELHI: Aam Aadmi Party (AAP) leader Arvind Kejriwal will address a town hall on E20 petrol policy during his two-day visit to Goa beginning Monday.Kejriwal landed at Dabolim Airport in south Goa on Sunday, where he was received by local party leaders.AAP sources said Kejriwal would address a press conference on Monday in Panaji and a town hall a day later on E20 petrol policy.The AAP, which has two MLAs in the 40-member Goa assembly, has announced a campaign against the "forced implementation" of E20 (ethanol-blended) fuel in the coastal state, which will go to polls in 2027.Earlier this month, Kejriwal had demanded the government give the option to choose between pure petrol and E20 at fuel pumps.The Centre introduced E20, a blend of 20 per cent ethanol and 80 per cent petrol, as part of efforts to reduce vehicular emissions and cut dependence on crude oil imports. After a phased rollout, E20 became the only petrol variant available at fuel pumps across the country from April 2026.
The move has triggered concerns among some vehicle owners, with motorists reporting reduced engine performance and apprehensions about damage to fuel lines and other components.
Owners of older vehicles, many of which may not have been designed for high ethanol blends, have been seeking availability of lower blends such as E10.Industry executives had sought to allay concerns over the use of E20 petrol, saying the fuel has undergone rigorous scientific testing, is safe for both new and older vehicles, and is critical for reducing India's dependence on imported crude oil.

2026-08-24

Kabir Adeniyi
24 August 2026•Update: 24 August 2026
 Amnesty International has urged Nigerian authorities to urgently secure the release of worshippers abducted during Friday prayers at a mosque in Niger State, warning of the growing threat posed by mass abductions across the country’s north.
The rights group made the call after terrorists attacked the Kpenya Mosque in Dekara District of Borgu Local Government Area on Aug. 21, abducting an unspecified number of worshippers.
Amnesty described the incident as particularly alarming because the victims were abducted while observing Friday prayers, adding that more than 80 people may have been taken during the attack.
“The Nigerian authorities must act urgently to ensure the safe rescue of the abducted worshippers,” the organization said in a statement Monday.
It also called on the government to address the broader wave of mass abductions targeting rural communities across northern Nigeria.
Amnesty said at least 1,100 people were abducted between January and April 2026, while noting that available figures vary and may significantly underestimate the actual scale of the problem.
The organization warned that victims of previous mass abductions had reported severe abuses, including torture, starvation, sexual violence and amputation.
The Niger State government confirmed the attack and said terrorists stormed the mosque while worshippers were observing Juma’at prayers.
State Commissioner for Information and Orientation Obed Nana said a joint security operation had been deployed to track the attackers and facilitate the rescue of the abducted worshippers.
“A joint security operation has been deployed to assess the situation, track the attackers, and facilitate the rescue of the abducted worshippers,” Nana told Anadolu.
The government declined to disclose further operational details, saying doing so could compromise ongoing rescue efforts, while urging residents to avoid spreading unverified figures.
The Kpenya attack occurred barely a week after an attack on worshippers in the Ungushi community in Sokoto State, where gunmen killed at least 32 people during Friday prayers, including the mosque’s imam.
Anadolu previously reported the Aug. 18 attack in Ungushi, highlighting growing insecurity affecting communities in northwestern Nigeria.
The latest incident adds to a series of attacks reported around the Borgu-Kainji corridor, an area where authorities and security analysts have raised concerns about armed groups operating around Kainji Lake National Park.

Tukaram Mundhe’s crackdown on poor hygiene and unsafe food is forcing businesses in Mumbai to make pre-emptive changes
Tukaram Mundhe is an Indian bureaucrat on a mission.
As a state food safety commissioner, the 51-year-old has raided food businesses in the country’s commercial capital Mumbai in an unprecedented crackdown on poor hygiene and unsafe food that has captivated the public nationwide.
Mundhe has led the suspension of food permits of four Domino’s and one Pizza Hut outlet in recent weeks, and has also targeted cricket clubs, hotels and well-known restaurants in over 3,000 raids since he took over as Maharashtra’s food safety chief in May.
When a Mumbai judge accused Mundhe of selective aggression towards private outlets and asked if court canteens were checked, his teams conducted inspections there within hours, shutting some of them which operated without licences.
“Those who try to not comply, they will face what is expected from the regulator,” Mundhe said in an interview with Reuters, talking about his no-nonsense approach.
“You are dealing with the lives of people.”

Defense Forces strike rare Russian counterpart to Starlink in Huliaipole area
The regiment reported this on Facebook, posting a video of the incident, according to Ukrinform.
The “Spirit-030” is a rare device that the Russians are using as a substitute for Starlink terminals. The antenna on this device has a diameter of only 30 cm, which makes it much harder to detect.
Russian troops began deploying “Spirit-030” terminals on a large scale after March of this year.
As reported by Ukrinform, Starlink verification was launched in Ukraine earlier this year, which restricted the operation of the terminals—only verified and registered ones continued to operate, while all others were shut down.
Without Starlink, Russia faced a catastrophe in its command and control of troops.
In August, it became known that Russia was accelerating the deployment of the “Rassvet” satellite constellation—its own counterpart to the Starlink system—ahead of the schedule it had set in its own plans.

Russia prioritizes oil, gas projects with India, hydrocarbon supplies — Manturov
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-08-24
14:44:35 UTC
23:44:35 KST

MOSCOW, August 24. /TASS/. Russia considers energy cooperation with India a priority, both in terms of supplies and joint hydrocarbon production and processing, Russian First Deputy Prime Minister Denis Manturov said at a meeting of the two countries’ intergovernmental commission on cooperation.
"We attach priority importance to cooperation in the field of energy security. This concerns not only supplies of oil, petroleum products, and coal, but also joint investment projects in hydrocarbon production and processing," he noted.
Earlier, Russian Energy Minister Sergey Tsivilyov said that India remained a key partner for Russia’s fuel and energy sector and that oil supplies remained at a high level. Russian Deputy Prime Minister Alexander Novak also noted that Russia was interested in attracting Indian investment in its oil and gas sector, as well as developing a distribution network in India with the participation of Russian companies.
Russia also sees potential for new joint projects with India in the extraction and processing of nonferrous, rare, and rare-earth metals, Manturov said. "We see potential for expanding cooperation in metallurgy and mining, including the joint development of deposits and the processing of nonferrous, rare, and rare-earth metals," he noted.
India will serve as the partner country of the international Russian Energy Week (REW) forum this year, becoming the first official partner country in the event’s history.
